- **Measurements are Def.8 probe files, not Go tests (R9, §3):** every EXECUTE item lands a dated `P3/measurements/` file (pre-registered expectation → observation → pass/fail); AC power held; honest blockers recorded per-measurement. `⚙ verification.entailment_sample_rate` is SET via an operator-owned `Registry.Set` write (override + audit), NEVER a code-default edit. **BENCH-REG is read-only** (its registered numbers change only via its own §17).
- **⚙ + walls + no-new-things (R31/R32):** consumed: `local.alias` (the swap gate is now its ONLY write surface), `local.batch.ac_only` (battery, first consumer), `local.vram.guard_band_mb`, `local.unload.term_grace_s`, `adapter.parallel_gate_fallback` (rider 2 reads the registry default). Referenced-not-consumed: `local.reeval.cadence_months` (v1 schedule surface). **NO new ⚙ key (118 tally green); ZERO new `components.lock` entries (17); migration 0010 is the only new one (immutable once committed).** `internal/local` stays storage/eventlog/gates/settings + stdlib; `memory`/`verify`/`worker` gain no `local` import (all AST-pinned, incl. the `internal/local/battery` local+stdlib posture, F11). The R14 order-preservation check is a hard ASSERT in the tier-L smoke. **The S12.10 deliberate-bump procedure has ONE named run-handle (F17): the `TestBumpProcedure*` set in `conformance_test.go` — `go test ./internal/local/ -run TestBumpProcedure` (add `SINET_LIVE_SMOKE=1` for tier L) runs pin↔lock coupling + the real llama-swap `/v1` logprobs/`json_schema`/unload contract + the R14 reason-first order assert as one battery (P-T15-2: behavioral, documentation never the check).**

## 29. Event-type contract & reconciliation conventions (P3-B5-1; S14.1–S14.2, S02.2/S03.1)

The S14.2 event-type contract is DATA in `internal/eventlog/contract.go` (the sole `run_events` owner — a new file, not a new package, not `internal/obs`; OQ3). It declares the **15 S14.2 families** with their **contract-minimum required fields verbatim** from the S14.2 table, every registered type's **family + `schema_version` (1 at v0) + reconciliation verdict + provenance**, the **`Classify(type)→(family,known)`** helper (S14.2 rule 3 / S03.1 forward-tolerance), and the **upcast-on-read hook** `Upcast(type,fromVersion,payload)` (S14.2 rule 2 / S02.1). The registry is data — it **never imports the producer packages** (they import `eventlog`; an import back would cycle, so the producer↔registry cross-check lives in the external `eventlog_test`). It adds **no runtime field validation**: `Append`/`validate` stay envelope-only (S02.1 validate-before-persist is the JSON + ⚙ `state.event_payload_cap` gate); required-field conformance is asserted at **test time** over representative payloads, so a B5-8 compaction-stripped payload (S14.9) never trips a runtime gate.

- **The standing rule (retires the "provisional pending S14" note):** every event type a producer mints MUST be registered in `contract.go` under exactly one family, or `TestDeclareOnce_EveryProducerTypeIsRegistered` fails. This one enforced assertion replaces the 20+ scattered "provisional pending the S14 event contract (B5)" comments (§7–§26), which are now retired-by-convention (not edited away one-by-one). A new minted type is a `contract.go` entry + a `producerTypes()` inventory entry in the same packet; a genuinely new *family* is an S00.9 amendment to frozen S14.2 (an operator act — the executor never edits `Spec/`).
- **Reconciliation verdicts** (recorded per type): `CONFORMS` / `RENAME` / `ADMIT` / `FIELD` / `DECLARE`. Families are **binding**; their S14.2 "v0 types" columns are **representative** — a family admits a type by scope. B5-1 reconciled **76 minted types** and declared **17 declare-only** future types (producers in B5-2/3/6/7/8 and B6): `stage.started/finished`, `run.parked/resumed` (= `run.state_changed` transitions, cause=park/resume), `ask.observed/answered` (projected from `asks`), `decision.recorded` (generic inbox), `watchdog.flagged/annotated/suppressed`, `drift.finding`, `canary.result`, `retention.compacted`, `tool.called`, `benchmark.pair_recorded`, `eval.score_recorded`, `run.summary_written`.
- **The rename set (11 → S14.2-canonical names):** `run.state`→`run.state_changed`, `run.checkpoint`→`checkpoint.written`, `context.manifest`→`knowledge.injected`, `orchestration.spawn`→`helper.spawned`, `orchestration.spawn_refused`→`spawn.refused`, `engine.usage`→`usage.recorded`, `engine.rate_limit`→`limit.event`, `verify.round`→`verdict.recorded`, `context.overflow`→`compaction.anomaly`, `deliverable.minted`→`artifact.produced`, `engine.tool_result`→`tool.completed`. Each changed the **constant VALUE** (emit sites reference the const → auto-update); the three engine renames route through `adapters.eventType(kind)` — `message`/`gate_ask`/`done` keep the `engine.<kind>` frame name, and `engine.gate_ask` STAYS the stored Gate/ask trace event (OQ5). **No `run_events` migration** (append-only; no production rows). `usage.recorded`/`limit.event` are STORED per-call records whose price-table-version [S10] and five-class limit-class fields are a field-addition seam for B1-2 metering, not built here; `ask.observed/answered` are the family's projected canonical types materialized by B5-2/B5-8.
- **Legacy-alias rule (R14):** every rename registers its OLD name as a `Classify` alias (old → same family) so any pre-existing dev/test `run_events` row still classifies — never fatal. This is upcast-on-read applied to type *names*: new writes use the canonical name, reads recognize both.
- **The broadened Platform scope (OQ2-(A) — a formalization, NO `Spec/` edit):** the **Platform** family reads as "platform-scope operational + asset-lifecycle events" and homes the B3/B4 machinery S14.2's 15 capability-families never enumerated — `worker.*` lifecycle (13, with `worker.compiled` → Routing), `registry.*` (4), `preview.*` (2), backup `platform.*` (3), `local.*` (4), and `meter.utilization` (→ Usage & limits). `auth.*` (9) admit by prefix under `auth.event`; `knowledge.*` (6, the S09 lifecycle side of S2.9) admit by prefix under `knowledge.injected`; the S02.2-named **`ledger_update`** keeps its underscore name as a documented dotting exception. This reading is documented in the family's `Scope` field, never a spec amendment.
- **Cross-packet ownership flags:** `run.wedged` is the recovery-side pause-and-flag marker (S02.5) — B5-3's `watchdog.flagged` is the watchdog side; two producers, two rows, B5-3 must not double-own wedged. `registry.drift` is project-topology drift (S13.7) under Platform — B5-6's Drift & canary (`drift.finding`) is outside-world drift; the name collision must not mis-route. **B5-2 SSE** frames `event:` = the stored canonical type (§7), so it consumes the canonical names, never the retired ones. **BENCH-REG stays read-only** — `benchmark.pair_recorded`/`eval.score_recorded` are declared here; their producers and numbers are B5-7 via BENCH-REG §17.
- **Test obligations (hermetic, stdlib `testing`):** declare-once (every producer type registered — non-tautological, proven by a scratch constant tripping it), reverse (every minted type has a producer), exactly-one-family, 15-families-with-verbatim-fields, `schema_version`=1, upcast-hook (a real v1 payload round-trips; a fixture type's registered v1→v2 fires), forward-tolerant (`Classify(unknown)`=false, and a reader over a `run_events` set with an unknown-type row does not fail), completeness (every S2.1–S2.11 / S14 Coverage capability maps to ≥1 registered family — a capability with no family is a contract defect, answered from `run_events` + in-substrate `checkpoints`/`asks`, never a side store), legacy-alias, and required-field conformance over representative goldens.

## 30. Live-inspection surface & codor-C2 redaction conventions (P3-B5-2; S14.3, S01.9, Research/18 §3-C2/§7-C2)

The one SSE endpoint `/events` (§7) is EXTENDED into the S14.3 live-inspection surface — topics/snapshot-then-tail/progress-semantics + serve-side redaction — never rebuilt. The frame contract of §7 is preserved: `id:` = `event_seq` (sole ordering authority), `event:` = the stored canonical type verbatim, resume via `Last-Event-ID` then `?after_seq`, short-batch 256/read on the 250 ms poll + nudge, keepalive every ⚙ `obs.sse_keepalive`. No new ⚙, no migration, no adoption.

- **The four topics are `{run, board, fleet, inbox}`** (`internal/api/topics.go`, constants). The topic map is DATA computed ABOVE the S14.2 families via `eventlog.Classify` (which resolves the 11 legacy aliases, so a pre-existing row still routes): `board` = RunLifecycle; `fleet` = Usage&limits + the S12 `local.*` seat types; `inbox` = GateAsk ∪ HumanDecision ∪ Watchdog ∪ DriftCanary; `run` = run-scope (any non-empty `run_id`). Tags are **multi-valued** — a `run.state_changed` carries both `board` and its `run`. Every delivered frame carries a `topics` field even on the unfiltered relay so the client routes it. A type in no topic never matches a topic filter (forward-tolerant). Platform-scope audit types (`settings.changed`, `auth.*`, `platform.*`) are in no household topic — they flow only on the unfiltered stream. Declare-only producers (`watchdog.*`, `drift.*`, `canary.*`, `stage.*`) are mapped now; their producers land in B5-3/B5-6/B6.
- **Subscribe/filter contract:** `?topics=<comma-list>` selects; `run` additionally requires `?run=<id>`; **no `?topics` = the unfiltered relay**; an unknown topic is a 400.
- **Snapshot-then-tail (OQ4):** a topic-subscribed (re)connect ALWAYS re-snapshots — the client's `Last-Event-ID` is SUPERSEDED. Capture the head cursor `H`, emit one `snapshot` frame per subscribed topic (`event: snapshot`, `data: {topic, cursor:H, state}`, no `id:`), then tail from `H`. The snapshot carries STATE, never event frames, so no frame is dropped or double-delivered across the boundary. The raw `?after_seq` **poll path stays delta-only** (the poller owns its cursor) — one code path, transport is a client detail (no `?once` single-shot was added; zero-new-code polling).
- **Progress semantics — NEVER percent-complete (a checkable NEGATIVE):** the run card (`internal/api/projection.go` `RunCard`) exposes FSM state incl. first-class `waiting_on_human` (parked ∧ open ask) and `parked_until`, current stage, current tool + args digest, monotonic counters (tokens, api-equivalent cost, elapsed, steps), the last-activity line, and the DERIVED `wedged`. There is NO `percent`/`fraction`/`ratio`/`progress`/`pct`/`complete_fraction`/ETA field anywhere — a conformance test asserts their absence; counters are monotonic and never imply a denominator.
- **`wedged` is DERIVED, never stored (R11):** true iff the most recent run-lifecycle marker for the run is the recovery-side `run.wedged` (a later transition supersedes it). It is NOT B5-3's `watchdog.flagged` — B5-2 surfaces the recovery side only; the two must not be conflated.
- **Owner-scoping per S01.9 AuthZ (OQ1):** every projection and the tail are owner-scoped in the data layer. The **operator** (the one role bit, read via the auth store — the dev-posture bootstrap identity is operator-equivalent for READ) sees ALL rows including platform-scope (`user_id='platform'`); a **member** sees only their own (`user_id == identity`). A member may subscribe to `run` only for their own run — 404 if missing, 403 if another owner's; the operator subscribes to any. This is IMPLEMENTING S01.9, not a discretionary behavior change; the B0-3 unscoped relay was a pre-multi-owner placeholder.
- **No engine SSE replay — standing conformance assertion (S14.5):** `/events` resume reconstructs the exact sequence from `run_events` ALONE (no engine/adapter in the loop; opencode ignores `Last-Event-ID`, fix declined upstream). The hermetic test `TestConformance_NoEngineSSEReplay_ResumeFromLogAlone` is the named seam; `TODO(S14.5/B5-4)` registers it into the `conformance_registry` table (which lands with B5-4).

**Codor C2 serve-side redaction** — `internal/redact` (a stdlib-only LEAF: `Redact(string) string`, `RedactJSON(payload) payload`), imported by `internal/api` now and by the B5-8 query/search layers later:

- **Targets = observability projections ONLY (§7-C2·1):** the primitive fires exactly at the SSE serving edge — every `/events` frame payload (`RedactJSON`) and every snapshot state (`RedactJSON` over the marshaled projection). It is NOT a blanket response middleware. Checkable: `redact.*` appears only in `internal/api/sse.go`; a test asserts the deliverable/review/preview/intake serve paths never import `internal/redact`.
- **Never deliverable/broker content (§7-C2·2):** S13 product content served by the review/preview/B6 deliverable paths is structurally exempt — those packages do not import the primitive.
- **Constant-on, no ⚙ (§7-C2·3):** never toggled, never a settings key. A future toggle would be one clamped S18 row under the standing settings-tab amendment — NOT this packet.
- **Store-raw / serve-redacted (R19):** the stored `run_events.payload` is NEVER mutated — redaction happens only at serialization. A planted secret is `[REDACTED:<class>]` on the wire and verbatim in the DB row.
- **Idempotent + honest-content-safe:** `Redact` runs on individual string VALUES (`RedactJSON` deep-walks the parsed structure and calls `Redact` per value, returning the input bytes unchanged when nothing was redacted — so honest frames are byte-for-byte stable). Every marker is inert (no rule re-matches its own output), so redacting redacted text is a no-op. Pattern classes (verified at use, mid-2026): PEM private-key blocks, Anthropic `sk-ant-…` (the load-bearing claude-cli lane), GitHub `gh?_`/`github_pat_`, generic `sk-…`, AWS `AKIA/ASIA`, `Bearer …`, uppercase `KEY=value` pairs, the `sinet_session` cookie. Patterns are conservative (prefix + length floor) so honest content is unchanged; over-redaction of the observability surface is acceptable, a leak is not.
- **Defense-in-depth, NOT a D2 substitute (§7-C2·4):** the doc comment and this section state plainly that redaction is a backstop for secrets INCIDENTALLY captured in tool output — it never replaces the D2 broker/scrubbed-env/key-hygiene prevention story, and no D2 posture is relaxed on its strength.
- **redact-before-match is B5-8's (R21):** the primitive is a pure text→text function reusable BEFORE a match, so B5-8's search redacts before matching (no oracle for redacted text) and bounds excerpts. **B5-2 builds NO search, history query, or compaction** — only the live stream + the reusable primitive.

## 31. Watchdog-suite conventions (P3-B5-3; S14.4, S12.4, S02.3, S10.8, S01.6–S01.8)

`internal/watchdog` is the S14.4 self-health suite: three tiers of detection over `run_events` + the local tier, costing no allowance, working when every paid window is empty. It imports `run`/`eventlog`/`metering`/`settings`/`storage`/`local` + stdlib; `internal/local` NEVER imports it (AST-pinned, `importwall_test.go`); `internal/api` reads its flags from `run_events` without importing it. The shell composes it and OWNS WHEN (three goroutines); the package owns the LOGIC.

- **HARD INVARIANT — NO auto-kill anywhere (S14.4 / G1 D1.3):** no check kills, terminates, tombstones, or gates a run — the watchdog contains-and-parks (pause-and-flag), always. Tier-1 ANNOTATES, never decides; Tier-2 is always a pause. `park` (running/draining→parked) is the ONLY watched-run transition; the synthetic dead-man canary self-terminates its OWN run via completed/finalized. `importwall_test.go` AST-proves the package references none of `StateTombstoned`/`StateCrashed`/`StateDiedAtGate`/`Kill`/`Tombstone`/`Terminate`/`SIGKILL`/`SIGTERM` and imports neither `os/exec` nor `syscall` (R16). It also (a) scans string BasicLits so `run.State("tombstoned")` cannot construct a forbidden state past the ident check; (b) allows the `StateCompleted`/`StateFinalized` idents ONLY in `deadman.go` (the canary's sanctioned self-termination) — any other file naming them fails; and (c) pins a FORWARD import allowlist `{eventlog,local,metering,run,storage}` so adding `scheduler`/`recovery`/anything new is a deliberate wall update, never a silent edge (the `internal/local` importwall precedent).
- **Tier 0 — deterministic, model-free, STATELESS (R1–R8):** the counters (loop `⚙ watchdog.loop_repeat`, ping-pong `⚙ watchdog.pingpong_cycles`, error-loop `⚙ watchdog.error_loop`, silence `⚙ watchdog.silence_budget.<run_type>`→`recovery.dead_after` seed, spend `⚙ watchdog.spend_median_mult`×median armed after `⚙ watchdog.spend_arm_days`, suspicious-completion) are PURE functions re-derived each evaluation from a bounded recent-events window per run (no durable cursor, no state table — S14.1). Every ⚙ is read by dotted key AT evaluation time (live-apply). The **behavioral** counters' recent-events read is **generation-fenced** to the run's CURRENT generation: pre-park events carry the old generation, so after an operator resume (parked→running bumps generation) the stale pre-park trace drops out and the run is NOT re-parked with zero new events; a genuine fresh anomaly (new events at the new generation) still flags. The fence is **behavioral-only** — the suspicious-completion check reads the whole run UNFENCED (across generations), so a run that did real work before a resume and then completed quietly is not mistaken for a silent failure. The watch set is a **POSITIVE state allowlist**: the behavioral counters watch `{running,draining}` only (a crashed run — `IsTerminal(StateCrashed)=false` — is NOT watched, so it never earns an unresolvable flag), and suspicious-completion watches the clean-completion terminal only (read by VALUE, never the `StateCompleted` ident). Silence is likewise a positive allowlist (`silenceWatched`): running/draining are watched unless waiting-on-human (an open ask); a parked run is silence-watched ONLY with a PAST resume-time (scoped to the limit-ish events — the park transition lands after the `limit.event` and must not mask `resets_at`); a resume-less park (watchdog/operator/gate/maintenance) is first-class waiting, never silence. The loop **signature** = a sha256 over tool name + args digest (never raw args, §30); an unsignatured payload never trips (the safe direction — honest for a non-conformant `tool.completed`, the B5-2 F13 carry). Error-loop keys on the ACTION (tool name), so a retry with drifting args still trips. Suspicious-completion has NO ⚙ — a conservative structural per-class floor (execution/verify roles ending with zero tools AND zero verdicts). The spend owner set is derived from the day's usage rows (`metering.SpendOwners`), not currently-active runs, so a person whose runs all completed today is still checked. Event-driven Tier-0 rides a shell-owned `Tail(cursor)` (the `After`+poll precedent); the time-based signals ride `Sweep`.
- **Tier 1 — local disambiguator, $0, annotate-only (R9–R14):** invoked ONLY on a Tier-0 behavioral trigger (loop/ping-pong/error-loop/suspicious-completion — where a transcript judgment is meaningful), via `local.Duty.Call` on the `watchdog-disambiguator` alias (`Classification:true`). The verdict schema (`WatchdogSchema()`) is built IN `internal/watchdog` (OQ6 — `internal/local` stays read-only), reason-first with the mandatory `unclear` abstain member (the `assertAbstain` belt enforces it). It records `watchdog.annotated` and NEVER gates: the verdict does not change whether Tier-2 parks (Tier-2 is ALWAYS); an error/abstain/unparse degrades to `unclear` (never a fabricated label); it never escalates beyond `unclear`, never a cloud fallback. The $0 D7 row lands ON the watched run when it is checkpointable (running/draining — trace coherence); a non-checkpointable (terminal/parked) watched run uses the `AdvisoryMeter` platform-run seam (OQ4); with neither, the annotation is skipped HONESTLY (logged), never a silent unmetered call. Tests are tier-F (`local.FakeServer`) — $0 by construction, no paid call ever; tier R/L is a `SANCTIONED SKIP`.
- **Tier 2 — pause-and-flag in ONE WriteTx (R15–R20):** the run parks via `run.Store.TransitionTx(...,StateParked,{Reason:"watchdog:<rule>",Actor:ActorPlatform})` and `watchdog.flagged` is appended in the SAME tx (§8; the park does not bump generation). Containment is STRUCTURAL — the scheduler admits no next paid call for a parked run (S10.8) — so NO `scheduler.ContainAndPark` seam is added (OQ3, the recovery-ladder direct-park precedent). A terminal/parked run is not re-parked (containment already holds); the flag stands alone. The flag carries {rule, anomaly_class, severity, signature, counts, spend_vs_baseline, annotation_ref} (satisfies the FamilyWatchdog descriptor).
- **Derive-from-log, NO side store (OQ2, R30):** open-flags, dedup-by-(run,class), and suppress-count-per-rule are all DERIVED from the `watchdog.*` rows in `run_events` — NO `watchdog_flags` table (migrations stay 0001–0010). An open flag = the latest `watchdog.flagged` per (run, class) not superseded by a `watchdog.suppressed` for the class or a resume (`run.state_changed` to=running — "resume, I was wrong"). A suppress matches the flag's FULL `anomaly_class`, so a suffixed run-less flag (`watchdog.spend:<owner>`, `watchdog.organ_absence:<organ>`, `watchdog.retune_proposal:<key>`) is clearable (its retune count folds into the base rule, split on the first `:`). The B5-2 inbox (`InboxSnapshot.WatchdogFlags`) is populated by the same derive-from-log query in `internal/api/projection.go`, owner-scoped per S01.9 (operator sees all incl. platform-scope; a member sees only own) — and it EXCLUDES the dead-man canary's `platform.deadman.*` flags (they would otherwise sit forever-open) and honors a PLATFORM-scope suppress of a run-less flag in a member's view (the clearing effect crosses; no other owner's flags are ever exposed).
- **Alert discipline (R21–R23):** two severities — flag-now (stalls/loops/spend on active runs, foreign-listener, dead-man) vs daily-digest (everything else) — computed per flag. Dedup one alert per (run, class), updates fold in. A sustained breach of `⚙ watchdog.flag_now_target`/day raises a digest-tier meta-alert (never a flag-now itself), excluding the daily canary's synthetic flags.
- **Suppress is a tuning signal (R19, OQ6):** `Suppress(actor, runID, rule)` emits `watchdog.suppressed` carrying the acting PRINCIPAL in the payload (a suppress is an operator act, not the platform's — §8 acting-principal-rides-the-payload); `rule` is the flag's full `anomaly_class` (suffixed for a run-less flag). On every `⚙ watchdog.suppress_retune_count`-th suppression of a ⚙-thresholded BASE rule it PROPOSES a threshold-raise admin card (a run-less digest flag rule=`watchdog.retune_proposal`, deduped per-⚙-key) — a PROPOSAL, never an auto-move (the ⚙ write stays operator-gated through the registry). The HTTP endpoint + React button are B6/S15.
- **Registered platform-health checks (R24–R27):** resume-reconcile (S01.7 wake completion), the recurring listener-binding audit, organ-absence (adopted organs down = degraded digest flags), and event-log-size (WAL + `run_events` growth past a structural threshold). The listener audit is a REAL in-process check (`auditOwnListeners`): it enumerates THIS process's sockets via `/proc/self/fd` and matches their inodes against the LISTEN rows of `/proc/net/tcp{,6}`, flagging any bound beyond loopback (flag-now High) — runtime drift the startup lint (a fixed config address) cannot see; the external front chain (tailscale serve / Caddy) runs in other processes and is out of this check's scope. Each check rides the sweep and consumes a nil-safe shell seam (honestly-absent at v0 where the host machinery — is-active from the sinet user, logind wake wiring — is a later packet). Each references the S14.5 conformance registry via `TODO(S14.5/B5-4)` — B5-3 does NOT build that table.
- **Dead-man canary V2 (R28-DMC, OQ5):** a SHELL-driven daily probe (the independent observer — non-circular) that (a) injects a synthetic loop trace on a `platform.deadman.*` canary run and asserts the FULL Tier-0→Tier-1→flag path fires, and (b) makes a $0 local liveness call. On failure → an alarm = `watchdog.flagged` (rule=`watchdog.dead_man`, flag-now — staying within the suite's own `watchdog.*` types, NOT poaching B5-6's `canary.result`); on pass → a quiet journald marker, no new event type. Distinct from the systemd `WatchdogSec` control-plane heartbeat. The canary self-terminates so it never lingers. DUENESS is derived from the event log (the last canary's birth), NOT a wall-clock ticker — the shell loop ticks on the sweep cadence and probes when ≥24h have passed OR no probe has ever run, so the daily probe survives restart and suspend (a ticker would reset on restart and freeze across suspend — the exact failure the dead-man guards); the 24h interval stays a structural constant.
- **Events + ⚙ + no-new-things (R29/R33/R37):** `watchdog.flagged/annotated/suppressed` are flipped declare→minted in `internal/eventlog/contract.go` (real provenance) + `producerTypes()` (79 minted / 14 declare-only); `run.wedged` is NOT touched (recovery-side; two producers, two rows). NO new ⚙ (the 8 `watchdog.*` keys consumed by dotted key; tally 118/33), NO migration (0001–0010), NO new dependency (`components.lock` 17). Structural constants (NOT ⚙): the sweep interval (30s), the dead-man daily cadence (24h), the Tier-0 tail poll (2s), the recent-events window (128), the suspicious-completion floor, the event-log-size threshold — the `sseBatchSize` precedent, interim under the settings-tab directive.

## 32. Conformance-registry conventions (P3-B5-4; S14.5, S02.1, S03.3)

`internal/conformance` is the S14.5 conformance registry — the control-plane **scheduling HOME** for every "proven, not assumed" obligation the spec declares (S14 term coinage line 7; body S14.5). It is a HOME, never an executor: it registers suites and drills, records their results, and computes dueness; it never runs a Go-test battery, spawns an engine, or dials anything (suite CONTENT stays with the declaring section — the weekly RUN of the adapter suites is B5-6's, the regression sweep B5-5's, CF6). Imports are `storage`+`eventlog`+`settings`+stdlib ONLY (AST-pinned, `conformance_test.go` `TestImportWall`, the §26/§31 precedent); it never imports a producer package it registers, nor `api`. `internal/api` surfaces red rows via its own projection query — reading the table without importing `conformance` (the §31 derive-from-log pattern).

- **Migration 0011 + the table (S02.1/§6):** `conformance_registry` (id, owning section, fixtures, trigger set, schedule + the two MUTABLE result fields last_run/last_result — control-plane state like `runs`/`asks`, not an event log). Platform-scope, owner-attributed (`user_id='platform'` CHECK, the 0009 `snapshot_ledger` shape) ⇒ inside the S02.9 durable set + every S13.10 archive by construction. Two triggers (the §22/§23 raw-SQL-battery pattern): `conformance_registry_structure_immutable` (`BEFORE UPDATE OF` every column EXCEPT last_run/last_result — a result update never rewrites identity/schedule/fixtures, R5) and `conformance_registry_no_delete` (a standing obligation is never dropped). Migration 0011 is immutable once committed; 0001–0010 byte-untouched.
- **Seed rows (S14.5 table verbatim; §14/§15/§17 in-code-seed precedent):** `SeedRows()` in code, seeded idempotently (`EnsureSeeded`, `INSERT OR IGNORE`) at shell boot — unconditional (platform obligations need no operator, unlike the S09.10 house seeds). Nine rows: the six spec row-groups with declared-by + triggers/schedule verbatim, the adapter row split **per-lane into the lanes whose suites EXIST** (anthropic = the claudecli battery, local = the S12.10 `TestBumpProcedure*`; the **zai lane is OMITTED with its reason recorded** — no adapter package, no v0 consumer, and a row claiming a nonexistent suite would fake, R7/§26–§27), plus the no-engine-SSE-replay assertion as its own row (its test lives in `internal/api`, CF2) and the dead-man canary drill (OQ5(c)). Every `fixtures` entry names a REAL in-tree test/procedure as DATA (the §28-F17 one-named-run-handle: package + test + env gates); resolution is source-scan-checkable (`TestSeedFixturesResolveToRealTests`), never an execution. Suites assert behavior, never docs (S03.3 rule 4) — the package contains no doc/help parsing.
- **`eval.score_recorded` minted at B5-4 (§29 flip mechanics):** flipped declare→minted in `contract.go` with real provenance + `producerTypes()` (79→**80** minted / 14→**13** declare-only), verdict `CONFORMS` (an S14.2-named canonical type — the B5-3 watchdog precedent), the family-14 header corrected. `benchmark.pair_recorded` stays declare-only (its numbers change only via BENCH-REG §17, CF5); B5-5/B5-7 are future co-producers of the family. `RecordResult` appends the event (platform-scope — a suite run has no platform run) AND updates last_run/last_result in ONE WriteTx (the §6/§8 state+event discipline; a failed record leaks neither). The payload is the family's contract minimum verbatim (suite id+version, asset id+version, metrics, runner+runner version; a representative golden joins the §29 conformance set, R11).
- **Red → card, derive-not-emit (S14.5; R14/R15/R16; OQ2/OQ3/OQ5):** a RED **recorded** row surfaces as an inbox card by derivation in `internal/api/projection.go` (`InboxSnapshot.ConformanceCards`) — NEVER a bare `asks` row (`asks.run_id` is NOT NULL; the B5-3 run-less-flag precedent), owner-scoped per S01.9 (platform-scope ⇒ operator-only; members never, R16). **flag-now vs digest** is row DATA (`affect_class`: kill-9/restore = storage, adapter/D6/compaction = lane ⇒ flag-now; forced-escalation/no-sse-replay = none ⇒ ordinary card) — an alert-routing class distinct from the inbox risk tiers. **GREEN raises nothing; overdue/never-run raises nothing at v0** (OQ3 — dueness is a passive read surface). **DERIVE rows** (restore-drill from `platform.restore_drill`, dead-man from the `platform.deadman.*` births + a `watchdog.dead_man` flag) resolve last_run/last_result from existing events by their canonical type STRINGS (NO import edge to `backup`/`watchdog`, OQ2/OQ5(c)); their table columns stay NULL so they never double-fire a conformance card — their own subsystems own the alert (mirror-not-re-emit).
- **Dueness = scheduling-home semantics (S14.5; R17; OQ4/OQ6):** derived from `schedule` + last-run, restart/suspend-safe (from stored state, never an in-memory ticker — the B5-3 dead-man precedent). Cadence tokens: structural `quarterly`=3 months / `weekly`=7 days / `daily`=24h (OQ6(a) structural row seed data, a 30-day month for the coarse passive surface; `eval.sweep_interval` is referenced-not-consumed — it is B5-5's S14.8 sweep, not registry dueness) and `setting:<dotted-key>:<unit>` read live at evaluation time (restore-drill = ⚙ `backup.drill_interval`; forced-escalation = ⚙ `verification.drill_interval_days`). The forced-escalation row additionally registers the daily escalation-canary schedule (⚙ `verification.canary_interval_hours`) as VISIBLE DATA only (OQ4(a)) — the canary RUNNER + `CanaryWatch.Silent` alert wiring stay B5-6/B6; `internal/verify` logic is untouched (comment-level pointer updates only). The S03.3 bump-gating tie is queryable DATA — `Store.BumpGatingRows` returns the rows whose trigger set carries `engine_bump`, and each such row's notes record the two-limb gate (a bump lands only after (a) the candidate passes its per-lane conformance suite AND (b) the S14.8 before/after quality probe shows no regression, P-T02-5) with its B5-5 reference (`bumpGateNote`) — no bump executor, no quality-probe machinery is built.
- **TODO discharge + no-new-things:** both `TODO(S14.5/B5-4)` markers are gone — `internal/api/sse_test.go` names its registered row (`no-engine-sse-replay`), `internal/watchdog/sweep.go` carries the OQ5(c) disposition comment (the four platform-health checks are continuous detection, S14.4-scheduled, NO rows; comment-only, the watchdog importwall unchanged). NO new ⚙ key (tally 118/33; the ⚙ keys read are all existing, by dotted key through the narrow `conformance.Settings`), NO new `components.lock` entry (**17 as of B5-4, 2026-07-25** — a HISTORICAL figure, corrected here at B5-8A per the coordinator's housekeeping disposition: the lock has since grown to **21** across B5-5/B5-6A/B5-6B, and the live gate is the `lockgate` count, never this line), NO new Go module, BENCH-REG untouched.

## 33. Regression-eval, revalidation-runbook & pinned-runner conventions (P3-B5-5; S14.8, S16.1–S16.4, S03.3, S07.9, S08.10, S12.9–S12.10)

`internal/evals` is the S14.8 home and the **EXECUTOR side** of the §32 registry (which is a scheduling HOME and runs nothing): per-asset eval objects, the per-version floor registry, the revalidation runbook, the settings-backed sweep cadence, the S03.3 limb-(b) quality probe, and the pinned-runner seam. Imports are `storage`+`conformance`+`verify`+stdlib ONLY (AST-pinned, `TestImportWall`, matched exact-or-prefix so a SUBPACKAGE is caught too, with a non-tautology probe): ⚙ values ride the package's own narrow `Settings` interface rather than an `internal/settings` import, and the event-log edge belongs to `conformance` (this package appends nothing directly). It NEVER imports `worker`/`local`/`stage`/`api`/`gates` — the flag, green-stamp and swap surfaces ride func seams composed at the shell root (`internal/shell/eval_seams.go`, the `FlagByModelFunc` precedent §28). The `verify` edge is the binding's: v0 software content IS `rubric-software` v2 + `golden-software` v1, so **no case content is invented** (growth stays S07.10 incident refresh + S08.6 dry-run pair seeding; research planted-citation suites are v0.1 and unbuilt).

- **promptfoo ADOPTED as the pinned runner (OQ1(a), the `sandbox-runtime` precedent verbatim):** lock entry `library` pin **0.121.19** (MIT, live-verified 2026-07-26 with the carve-out scan), **NO `modules` claim** (a Node CLI, not a Go module) and **no npm-side `modules` mechanics** (§4 defines that at B6). Consumed UNMODIFIED as an external subprocess behind `evals.Runner`; discovery = PATH or `SINET_PROMPTFOO_PATH`; the exact-version `npm install -g promptfoo@0.121.19` is a **B5-gate HOST act — this packet installed nothing**. CI is hermetic on a **stub binary under `t.TempDir()`**; the real leg auto-runs when installed, version-asserts the pin, and prints `SANCTIONED SKIP (CONVENTIONS §10)` otherwise. npx-per-invocation was rejected (a floating supply-chain surface on every cold run) and a container was rejected (no official image is documented — live-checked). **DeepEval** is the `standby` entry (observed 4.1.3/Apache-2.0, pin-on-activation, the Ollama-row shape) with the honest cross-ecosystem note; lock count 17 → **19**, #10 operator ratification **queued for the B5 gate**.
- **Runners-never-stores is STRUCTURAL (P-T11-4):** the adapter builds an explicit child env (deny-by-default), sets `PROMPTFOO_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` + `PROMPTFOO_DISABLE_UPDATE=1`, passes `--no-share --no-progress-bar --no-table`, and redirects `PROMPTFOO_CONFIG_DIR`/`PROMPTFOO_CACHE_PATH` into a per-run scratch dir removed after the run — **`Identity()` runs under the same isolation as `Run`**, and the hardening block is applied LAST with any colliding caller `RunConfig.Env` entry DROPPED, so no caller can weaken it (these are adoption-walk invariants of the entry, not caller conventions) — promptfoo's own eval state is **never read back as a record**. Exit **100** is "a test failed" (a legitimate red run), exit 1 is an error. The parser accepts `results.version` **3** and refuses any other version loudly (schema recorded from `examples/simple-cli/output.json` at the pinned tag); the generated config is JSON emitted into promptfoo's default `.yaml` config name (JSON is a subset of YAML, so Sinet stays stdlib-only). All four facts are pin-coupled and re-verified on every bump.
- **ONE recording home (OQ2(a)):** the new `regression-eval-sweep` registry row (§32 seed, cadence `setting:eval.sweep_interval:months`) is where **every** S14.8 result lands through `conformance.RecordResult` — per-asset results carry the asset in the payload's asset id/version, **one aggregate sweep result records LAST** (red if any asset red) so the row's resting state IS the sweep verdict, and the limb-(b) probe records against the SAME row with the **engine** as asset and the **candidate pin** as asset version (keeping the per-lane adapter rows purely (a)-limb). The row closes the loop `bumpGateNote` opened. **No new event type** — B5-5 is a co-producer of the minted `eval.score_recorded` (inventory stays 80/13). A red row raises the owner card through the **existing B5-4 derivation** (`internal/api` projection, operator-scoped) — never a bare `asks` row.
- **⚙ `eval.sweep_interval` is now genuinely CONSUMED** (it was referenced-not-consumed at B5-4): by dotted key through the narrow `evals.Settings`, read live, with the registry's own passive dueness reading the same key through its cadence token — a standing test asserts the two agree. Dueness derives from stored state (the row's `last_run`), so it is restart/suspend-safe; **no ticker exists**. Tally stays **118/33**; no new key.
- **Migration 0012 (immutable once committed; 0001–0011 byte-untouched):** `eval_floors` — floors keyed **(asset id, asset VERSION)**, registered ONCE per version (re-floor = a new version) and immutable except the `ratified` flip, plus a no-delete trigger; `revalidation_stamps` — append-only dated stamps (who / when / which suites / vs which baseline), no-update and no-delete triggers, FK to `worker_template_versions`.
- **Seed floors are MEASUREMENT-DERIVED (OQ5(a)), operator ratification queued for the B5 gate:** `rubric-software` v2 catch floor **0.84** = the Wilson 95% LOWER bound of the measured 20/20 (a point-estimate floor would red the rubric on any single miss at n=20); TNR **0.19** is carried **report-only** — a TNR floor would flag the judge for being conservative, the over-strict direction the B4 gate accepted as safe. Both are named code constants (S18 ratifies no key — the §7/§9 precedent, under the standing settings-tab directive). Floors register at the **8.3 knowledge-gate ENTRY POINT** — the same boot step that re-homes the B2 seed objects under S09.10 governance, not a second gate — unconditionally, because floors are platform data and need no D10 holder. An **unfloored** version is not evaluable and therefore not green (BENCH-REG §10.1(d)); limb-(d) evaluation/display stays B5-7's.
- **Comparison is PER SLICE, never an average (S08.10(a); R15 §2.4):** `Compare` reds the asset when any non-report-only slice regresses below its pinned baseline or falls below its registered floor. The planted-defect suite is the **slice guard** — its red is fatal even when every other slice improves. The pinned baseline for a rubric is read off the bundle's OWN `GoldenSetRates`, so the runbook and the S07.11 verdict records can never disagree.
- **The runbook accepts EXACTLY the spec's three triggers and re-implements nothing:** an **engine bump** flags through `FlagByEnginePin` (S08.10(b)/P-T14-1, with S03.3 step 5 as the announcement clock); a **model/duty-alias swap** CONSUMES the flag set the S12.10 swap gate already produced before the alias went live (P-T15-1 — a second gate is forbidden); a **watchlist drift finding** is accept-shape only (the emitter is B5-6's, and no emitter is faked). A fourth kind is refused; an unwired surface fails loudly.
- **The green stamp is a worker-lifecycle verb** (`worker.Store.Revalidate`): a flagged version returns to active ONLY through a dated revalidation record, in ONE WriteTx with the status flip and a `worker.validated` event marked `revalidation:true` — **no new event type**, and no second approval (approval and guardrails stand, §18). A red stamps too and changes nothing: the version stays flagged, supervised-only.
- **P-T06-5 is enforced at the ONE judge-construction site (OQ3(a)):** `verify.UnsupervisedJudgingGate` is a PURE function over the rubric bundle's own `JudgePin` + `GoldenSetRates` (model-id match at a token boundary, so a prefix never satisfies a pin); `internal/verify` gains **no import**. `stage.newVerifier` consults it and returns an error, so no verdict can be minted under an unvalidated judge; the gate reads `judge.Meta().Model` — the model that ACTUALLY judges and that the verdict record publishes. An injected `Judge` is the composition root's own dev/test seam (the nil-`Confiner` precedent) and the shell injects none, so production always passes through the gate. The runbook owns the re-measurement (the committed B4-7 rider-1 harness, recorded under its own runner identity) and the rubric version bump that clears the block.
- **S12.9 × the T15 battery (OQ6(a), ratified as a coordinator READING — no S00.9):** "via the pinned eval runner" means the S14.8 runner **FAMILY**, which includes the gate-validated in-repo Go-native battery (`t15-v1`, engine-build-keyed). The connective obligation is **RECORD-THROUGH**: `local.SuiteResult` lands as `eval.score_recorded` via `evals.Store.RecordLocalSuite` (a mirror struct — no import either way), with the seat as asset and the engine build as asset version. Re-plumbing the battery as promptfoo configs is FORBIDDEN — it would duplicate a validated instrument and reverse a closed gate decision. The battery owns its acceptance bar; this package records the verdict and never invents one.
- **The limb-(b) probe content is QUALITY-shaped, never schema-shaped (P-T02-5):** `SeedProbeSuite` (`bump-probe-v1`) is five small REAL tasks whose expectations are quality signals and whose forbidden strings are the effort-drift tells (stub / not-implemented / refusal / a banned dependency / scope creep). The machinery + fake-runner tests land now; a REAL before/after run needs a live candidate engine and is a coordinator act, not packet-mandatory. **No bump executor is built and the S03.3 policy text is untouched.**
- **Paid posture: this packet spent $0.** The first REAL sweep leg is PRE-REGISTERED (golden 26×2 on `claude-opus-4-8`, projection **$2.10**, stop line **$5.00** — the operator-ratified rider-1 class number) and executes only on coordinator say-so at landing or gate.

## 34. Watchlist-executor conventions (P3-B5-6A; S14.6, S16.1–S16.4/S16.8, S10.2–S10.3, S10.5, S14.4)

`internal/watchlist` is the S14.6 watchlist executor — the machinery that detects outside-world change (formats, limits, billing status) **before failures or costs spread**. This packet is the **A half** of an A/B split taken at the event-type seam: it owns everything that produces **`drift.finding`**; the API canary layer (`canary.result`: auth / behavioral / logprob / model-list) is the sibling half and is NOT built here. The package owns the LOGIC; the SHELL owns WHEN (one goroutine, the WAL/recovery/watchdog-loop precedent, §7/§8/§31).

- **Package map:** `watchlist.go` (types, ⚙ seam, the S14.4 severity rule), `rows.go` + `store.go` + migration **0013** (`watch_rows`), `seed.go` (the seed set + strict-JSON `LoadRows`/`WriteSeed`), `fetch.go` (conditional GET), `feed.go` (Atom/RSS), `cdio.go` (the changedetection.io driver), `aggregator.go` (structural JSON diff), `prices.go` (the vendored baseline + refresh-as-proposal), `classify.go` (the local second pass), `drift.go` (the emitter), `decay.go` (P-T11-3), `executor.go` (one pass over the due rows). **Import wall** (AST-pinned, exact-or-prefix, non-tautology probe): storage/eventlog/local/metering + stdlib only. It NEVER imports `api`/`adapters`/`worker`/`stage`/`verify` — and deliberately not `evals` or `settings` either: ⚙ values ride the package's own narrow `Settings` interface, and the S14.8 revalidation edge rides a func seam composed at the shell root (`internal/shell/watchlist_seams.go`), so the `evals→verify→intake→adapters` graph stays out of an observability leaf (the §28 `FlagByModelFunc` / §33 eval-seams precedent).
- **Migration 0013 — the watch-row config store** (S14.6 ¶1 rows `{url|feed, type, parser-hint, lane|candidate}`; G2 Def.12's "no new DB" means no second STORE, not no table). Platform-scope `user_id='platform'` CHECK (the 0009/0011 shape) ⇒ inside the S02.9 durable set and every S13.10 archive by construction. Structure is immutable by trigger (`BEFORE UPDATE OF` every column except the fetch-state set — the `conformance_registry_structure_immutable` shape) and a no-delete trigger enforces the S16.2 removal discipline: **a watch row leaves only by a dated funeral edit**. Both walls are proven by a raw-SQL attempted-violation battery. Mutable fetch state: `last_fetch_at`, `last_etag`, `last_modified`, `last_content_hash`, `seen_entries`, `structural_baseline`, `fail_streak`, `decay_stage`, `external_ref`. Migrations `0001`–`0012` stay byte-untouched.
- **Seeds are in code, seeded unconditionally and idempotently at shell boot** (`INSERT OR IGNORE`; platform obligations need no D10 holder — the §32 precedent, not the S09.10 house-seed precedent). Content: the **report-02 §6 tiered list** (Tier 1 pricing/plan pages + provider release-notes · Tier 2 repo canary feeds · Tier 3 aggregator APIs · Tier 4 hnrss + `r/LocalLLaMA/.rss`), **every S16.8 standing row**, the **G4 `[schema]` STUDY row** (source `schema-harness.github.io`, the report-12 discipline caveat verbatim in `notes`), the **awesome-harness-engineering row carrying BOTH referents** (`walkinglabs/…` + `ai-boost/…`, drop-if-both-stall — ONE row, because G2 Def.15 ratified one drop decision, not two watches), and **one review row per `components.lock` entry** (S16.4 #9; `watchlist.LockRowID` is the shared derivation, and a test asserts coverage in both directions so a new adoption without its watch row fails the build). Every row cites its ratifying source in `notes`; operator editing is strict-JSON `LoadRows` (an unknown field, bad cadence/kind/tier or duplicate id fails LOUDLY) with `WriteSeed` for gate review.
- **No fabricated URL.** Where a ratifying source elides the concrete endpoint (the awesome-harness referents, the Artificial Analysis API which also needs an operator key) the row is seeded as a `study` registration with the gap stated in `notes`, to be activated by a `LoadRows` override. GitHub publishes Atom for releases and commits but **not** for issues, so report-02's "issues" leg rides the commits/releases feeds plus the tier-1 pages — a guessed feed URL would decay on its first fetch. **hnrss is consumed as HOSTED, never self-hosted** (no license file), recorded on every row that uses it.
- **The P-T11-3 migrate bias is ROW DATA** (`migrate_bias`), set on every scrape target and surfaced on the decay card — a spec-named posture, not a comment.
- **changedetection.io ADOPTED** (`components.lock` `organ-unit`, pin **`0.55.8`**, Apache-2.0 **path-scoped by this packet's own carve-out scan at the pinned ref** — 1192 paths, single root LICENSE, no `enterprise/`/`ee/`, no dual-license trailer; named `replacement` + bus-factor-tightened `abandonment`; non-empty `watch`; **no `modules` claim** — a Python service). Integration is **REST + config ONLY**: no upstream code is vendored, patched or forked anywhere in the diff. Sinet's rows are the **source of truth** — reconciliation creates, rewrites and **deletes** Sinet-tagged organ watches, so a hand-edit in the organ's UI is reconciled away; watches outside Sinet's tag are never touched. Discovery is `SINET_CDIO_URL` + `SINET_CDIO_API_KEY` (structural deployment config, the `SINET_SRT_PATH` precedent); the API key is broker/`engine-cred`-class and is never logged, marshaled, or placed on a struct reaching a park record or the DB. **The host install is a B5-gate operator act — this packet installed nothing and changed no host state.** CI is hermetic against an `httptest` stub serving the route shapes read from the pinned source; an AST string-literal tripwire forbids any routable URL literal in a test file; the real leg prints `SANCTIONED SKIP (CONVENTIONS §10)`. **The S16.4 ten-check walk is recorded in the packet report; #10 (operator approval) is QUEUED FOR THE B5 GATE.** Lock count 19 → **21**.
- **Feed poller: stdlib only, zero new Go modules.** Conditional GET (`If-None-Match`/`If-Modified-Since` replayed from stored validators; a `304` is the cheap no-change path), content-hash dedup over the NORMALIZED body (CRLF folded, trailing whitespace trimmed — a cosmetic line-ending change is not a hit), plus per-ENTRY hash dedup so a re-published item is not raised twice. Atom + RSS are `encoding/xml` structs, **forward-tolerant**: unknown elements and namespaces are ignored, and a malformed feed is a **fetch failure, never a panic and never fatal** (the S03.1 discipline applied to feeds). Bounded reads, explicit timeouts, bounded redirects, and an explicit contactable `User-Agent`.
- **Aggregator diffs are STRUCTURAL, never free text:** the baseline is a compact leaf-path → value-hash digest, so added/removed/**changed** keys are all derivable without keeping the document. Over the digest cap the baseline degrades **honestly** to the whole-body hash and the finding says the diff is coarse. A first observation establishes the baseline QUIETLY — there was no prior state to have drifted from.
- **genai-prices refresh ALWAYS lands as a PROPOSAL** [G2 Def.16; S10.3; P-T08-3]. The pinned `prices/data.json` is **vendored** content-hashed at `internal/watchlist/pricedata/` with the upstream MIT LICENSE beside it (S16.2), a `data` lock entry whose pin carries the hash, and a test asserting the bytes still hash to the pin. Its **one** consumer is the diff baseline: the loader / user overlay / persistence stay S10.3's metering follow-up. Proposed rows carry real `metering.PriceRow` values **with effective dates**, per-single-token, scoped to lanes Sinet actually prices (`anthropic`, `zai`) with out-of-scope and conditional/tiered upstream shapes REPORTED rather than dropped or flattened. **No code path applies a price change and none auto-flips a billing-regime flag** (S10.2/S14.6) — asserted by test and by source scan. Staleness past ⚙ `adoption.price_data_stale_days` **proposes** the S16.8 LiteLLM-primary fallback; the clock derives from the log, no side store.
- **Second pass on the local tier, $0, honest in every degradation:** `local.Duty.Call` on `local.AliasWatchlist` with `Classification: true` and a schema built HERE (the B5-3 OQ6 precedent — `internal/local` stays read-only), reason-first with the mandatory `unclear` abstain member. A watch hit has no run, so every call rides the `AdvisoryMeter` platform-run seam (§28/§31); with no meter the pass is **skipped honestly**, never issued unmetered. An absent stack, an error, an abstain or an unparseable answer yields "unclassified — the operator reads it": the hit still becomes a card, and a label is never fabricated.
- **Severity is DERIVED, never judged.** S14.4 fixes the rule mechanically, so the model annotates `{lanes, change class, summary}` while `severity` is computed server-side from the change class and the **watch ROW's** lane: flag-now for an active lane's price/terms/limits/models/endpoints and for ANY billing-regime change; daily digest otherwise. A hit's own body never decides its lane or its severity.
- **`drift.finding` MINTED** (the §29 flip: real provenance in `contract.go` + a `producerTypes()` entry in the same packet; inventory **81 minted / 12 declare-only**), verdict `CONFORMS`, platform-scope (`UserID: "platform"`, no `RunID`), `SchemaVersion: 1`, payload satisfying the `FamilyDriftCanary` minimum verbatim and joining the §29 required-field golden set. `canary.result` stays declare-only — it is the sibling half's to mint. **`registry.drift` is untouched** and a test asserts the two are never conflated. `internal/api/topics.go` needed **no edit**: the existing family map already routes drift to `inbox`, proven by test.
- **Cards DERIVE from the log — no card table, no bare `asks` row** (`asks.run_id` is NOT NULL; the B5-3 run-less-flag / B5-4 conformance-card precedent). `InboxSnapshot.DriftCards` is populated by a query over `drift.finding` in `internal/api/projection.go`, owner-scoped per S01.9 (platform-scope ⇒ operator-only; a member never sees them), with `internal/api` importing nothing from `internal/watchlist` (AST-asserted). **Fingerprint-per-incident-window dedup — one card per storm:** the fingerprint is a stable derivation over `{source, lane, change class}`, and a finding folds into the open card while it falls inside the window of that card's FIRST hit; a later hit opens a new card. A non-conformant payload is skipped, never fatal (S14.2 rule 3).
- **The S14.8 revalidation edge — coordinator disposition OQ4(a), and the point is VISIBILITY:** the runbook fires **only** for change class `models` carrying a **model-id** subject, because the only wired hook (`worker.Store.FlagByModel`) matches a model id against `validation_records.model` and the template's `Profile.ModelPin`. Every other class raises its card and **records on the card that no revalidation was triggered, with the reason**; a `models` finding with no model subject records the same. No `internal/evals` change, no faked flag, and a runbook failure is recorded without ever suppressing the card. A test asserts non-model classes do not reach the hook at all. **6A's own sources (feeds, page diffs, structural key diffs) name no model id** — the producer that can is the sibling layer's model-list canary, and the `Hit.Subject` seam is already exercised end to end.
- **Decay posture (P-T11-3):** a fetch-failure streak reaching ⚙ `watchlist.fetch_fail_streak` (read by dotted key at evaluation time — live-apply proven by a test that moves the value mid-run) raises a card and advances the ladder **plain → browser fetcher → flagged-decayed-with-an-alternative-proposed**. The second rung is real only where it can be: at pin 0.55.8 the organ's browser leg is an **external CDP service the operator selects with `PLAYWRIGHT_DRIVER_URL`** (playwright is not one of its dependencies), so Sinet requests `fetch_backend: html_webdriver` as CONFIGURATION and never claims a browser exists — and a feed or API row, having no browser rung, goes straight to decayed. **A dead watch MUST announce itself**, and the card names the ⚙ key that fired it. A failing source is retried on its cadence, never in a hot loop.
- **Dueness derives from STORED state** (`last_fetch_at` + the row's cadence), never an in-memory ticker, so a pass resumes correctly across a restart and a wall-clock jump (proven by a test that rebuilds the store and jumps the clock). The shell loop ticks on a structural interval and asks which rows are due; **no ticker drives a row's cadence**. A study row is never polled — it is a standing review obligation surfaced through `PendingReview`, the read surface the S16.7 quarterly pass consumes, whose outputs are PROPOSALS, never silent bumps (R21).
- **Organ liveness goes live:** `watchdog.Config.Organs` was nil until this packet and is now wired to a `GET /api/v1/systeminfo` probe, so a down organ becomes a `watchdog.organ_absence:watchlist` degraded digest flag. An installed-vs-pin delta is reported **LOUDLY** in the probe note and never silently retargeted (§10). With the page tier absent the feed and API tiers keep working — proven by test.
- **The units carve-out extends** (§7 "generated, never installed"): changedetection.io ships **no** unit file — a recursive tree read at tag 0.55.8 matched no `*.service` and no `systemd/` path — so `sinet-watchlist.service` IS generated (`Draft: true`), exactly as `sinet-llamaswap.service` is, because the unit is Sinet configuration for an unmodified adopted binary (S16.1 config-only integration, not a fork). Its `ExecStart` runs the **operator-installed** organ, never the sinet binary, and binds **loopback** — the organ's own default host is `0.0.0.0`, so `-h` is load-bearing, not cosmetic. The B0-era `internal/units` package doc listing "the watchlist executor" among organs shipping their own units was an unchecked assumption and is **corrected** here; that is a repo-doc correction under an existing precedent, not an S00.9 amendment.
- **Structural constants, each with its reason** (S18 ratifies no key — the §7 `sseBatchSize` / §9 auth-constant precedent, interim under the standing settings-tab directive): the cadence tokens and their intervals (`continuous` 15 min — conditional GET makes a no-change poll a 304, so ~15 feeds cost essentially nothing; `weekly` 7 d; `monthly` 30 d; `quarterly` 3 months, the same coarse month `internal/conformance` uses), the shell loop interval (1 min — the *tick*, never the cadence), the drift incident window (24 h), the fetch timeout / body cap / redirect cap, the feed excerpt and finding detail caps, the seen-entry cap, the structural-digest cap, the proposed-row cap, and the generated unit's binary/datastore/listen paths.
- **⚙ discipline:** exactly two keys consumed, by dotted key, read live: `watchlist.fetch_fail_streak` and `adoption.price_data_stale_days`. Referenced-not-consumed and named in a comment so nothing hardcodes them: `scheduler.missed_slot_default` (the v1 schedule surface — at v0 the only schedules are platform-internal timers), `adoption.dependency_pass_months` / `adoption.abandonment_months` (S16.7 owns its own cadence), and `canary.auth_interval` / `canary.behavioral_interval` (the sibling canary layer's — a test asserts this package does not consume them). **NO new ⚙ key**: the tally stays **118 keys / 33 domains**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged. **No new Go module** — `go.mod` unchanged.
- **Coordinator dispositions, recorded:** **OQ1(a)** host install + a Sinet-generated `sinet-watchlist.service` under the B4-5 carve-out, plus the `units` package-doc correction. **OQ2(b)** poll-first — Sinet polls `GET /api/v1/watch` and `/difference/<from>/<to>` on the R20 loop and the organ's `json://` notifications go unused: **no inbound route, no new secret, no new auth class** (S14.3 already sanctions polling). The push ingress is **pre-registered** as the upgrade and its shape is now KNOWN, not guessed: apprise 1.9.9's Custom-JSON POST carries exactly `{version, title, message, attachments, type}` with `:key=value` URL args merging extra keys into the same top-level object, and changedetection.io Jinja-renders `{{watch_uuid}}`/`{{watch_url}}`/`{{diff}}`/`{{llm_intent}}`/`{{llm_summary}}` into body, title AND url — so (a) lands as a scoped follow-up whenever its auth class is ratified. **OQ3(a)** vendor + materialize the `data` entry + build the refresh/proposal job; the loader/overlay stay metering's. **OQ4(a)** as above. **OQ5** belongs to the sibling canary half and is untouched here — with it, B5-4's "dueness raises nothing at v0" narrows to "**except the two weekly lane-affecting adapter rows**"; `internal/conformance` is unchanged by this packet.
- **Live verification is a build-time obligation, not a memory exercise.** Every pinned fact in this section was read from the pinned ref at build time (2026-07-26): the version, licence and tree contents at tag `0.55.8`; the 13 REST routes, their status codes and the `x-api-key`/403 auth behaviour from `changedetectionio/api/` at the tag; the per-watch `llm_intent` field and its resolution from `changedetectionio/model/__init__.py` + `llm/evaluator.py` — **watch → the first tag carrying an `llm_intent` → `('', '')`, i.e. NO global fallback and no evaluation at all** (`resolve_intent` returns empty and the triage simply does not run; an earlier draft of this section wrongly wrote "watch→tag→global", corrected at the drain), which is what makes Sinet's per-watch `llm_intent` load-bearing rather than decorative; the browser fetcher's external-CDP mechanism from `content_fetchers/`; the apprise `json://` payload keys from `apprise/plugins/custom_json.py` at `v1.9.9`; the CLI flag set from the organ's `getopt` string; and the genai-prices tree, licences and document shape at `v0.0.72`. Suites assert BEHAVIOR against the stub, never documentation (S03.3 rule 4).

### §34 drain round 1 (P3-B5-6A, 19 evaluator findings — 15 actioned, 4 dropped/carried)

The landing battery was green and hermetic but blind in one place that mattered, and honest-looking in several that were not. What changed:

- **The gzip bug (D1), the packet's real defect.** `fetch.go` hand-set `Accept-Encoding: gzip`, which **disables** net/http's transparent negotiation: the transport returns the RAW compressed body and every parser downstream is handed gzip magic. Live-probed origins in the seed set — hnrss.org, models.dev, **raw.githubusercontent.com (the genai-prices refresh source itself)** and openrouter.ai — all answer that header with `Content-Encoding: gzip`, so in production the four hnrss rows and openrouter would have failed every cycle into decay within three, the price refresh would have failed `BuildPriceProposal`, and models.dev would have hashed compressed bytes so its baseline never formed. The hermetic stubs served identity, which is precisely why the whole suite passed. The header is gone (the transport negotiates and decompresses) plus a belt that gunzips an UNSOLICITED `Content-Encoding: gzip`. Locked by a stub serving real gzip in both shapes, and by a source-scan negative so the header cannot come back.
- **Reddit serves Atom at `/.rss` (D2).** The row hinted `rss`, and an explicit hint is HARD-ROUTED with no fallback, so a source S14.6 ¶T2 names by hand was seeded to fail forever. Hint corrected; the `ParseFeed` doc no longer claims "a mis-hinted row still parses" — that is true only for an *unrecognized* hint, and both semantics are now pinned by test.
- **R9's two halves separated honestly (D3).** The half the pinned REST surface CAN express is now driven: `include_filters` + `subtractive_selectors` on `WatchSpec`, with a generic page-chrome strip applied to every page watch. Per-site include filters stay EMPTY deliberately — a guessed CSS selector would silently filter away the pricing table the row exists to watch, so the mechanism ships live and the selectors are bring-up data. The half it CANNOT express is recorded with its evidence: `LLMSettings.api_base`/`.model` are GLOBAL organ settings and **no** route at 0.55.8 touches them (13 `add_resource` registrations, and a grep of `flask_app.py` for `LLMSettings`/`api_base`/`application_settings` matches nothing), so endpoint pointing is an operator install-time step carried as `LocalEndpointConfig` data for the gate proposal.
- **First-boot flood and the relevance filter (D4).** Feed rows now establish their baseline on FIRST observation without raising cards — the identical rule `pollAPI` already applied, for the identical reason (a feed's existing window predates the watch), which on a fresh boot was worth on the order of a couple hundred cards and a local classify call each. Separately, a hit the second pass classifies `none` is no longer a finding at all: S14.6 ¶2 says RELEVANT hits become drift cards, and the second pass answering "irrelevant" IS that filter. The projection carries the same exclusion as a belt for rows written before the rule. A price refresh proposing zero priced rows likewise raises nothing.
- **Severity could never be flag-now where it mattered most (D5).** Severity derived from the row lane alone, so the `t2-opencode-*` rows — which report-02 calls the strongest cross-provider canary and which carry NO lane by design — routed to daily digest even when the classifier's own payload named an active lane, so the card displayed a lane its severity contradicted. An empty row lane now falls back to the classified lanes; a row WITH a lane still wins (server-side truth).
- **The deviation's own junction is now tested (D6).** The five lines the import-wall deviation rests on were tested only for nil, so a Subject/Reason swap or a wrong `TriggerKind` would have passed everything. A real `*evals.Runbook` over a spy now receives a models-class finding end to end, with `FlagByEnginePin` wired as a tripwire for the wrong trigger kind, plus the non-model negative through the same composed seam. A minimal `SecondPass` interface was introduced on the emitter so these derivations are drivable without a local stack.
- **Exact migration pins restored (D7).** Two brief-§6 "never touched" files were touched, and worse, weakened from `== 12` to `>= 12`. Both are back to EXACT (`== 13`) — a floor would let an unnoticed migration slip in — and both touches are declared here and in the packet report.
- **The Miniflux deferral is recorded (D8)** in the file it would replace, with the ratifying gate, why the native poller did not prove gnarly, the HMAC drop-in contract, and the re-entry condition. No `standby` entry is materialized: pinning a component the funeral plan does not yet need is not free.
- **The override has a door (D9).** `LoadRows` had no production consumer. It is now loaded at composition from `SINET_WATCHLIST_ROWS`, **additively** — an override can add rows but can never delete a standing obligation by omission — and a present-but-malformed file fails the boot LOUDLY. Two seed rows promised activation "through a `LoadRows` override" *of that row*, which `INSERT OR IGNORE` plus trigger-immutable structure makes impossible; both now say what is actually true (the operator adds a row alongside; editing a committed one is an S16.2 dated funeral edit).
- **Drift cards are bounded (D10).** A drift finding has no close verb at v0 (dismiss is B6's), so every finding ever logged derived into a forever-listed card — unlike OPEN-only watchdog flags and RED-only conformance rows — and a re-firing condition grew the inbox without limit. Cards are now horizon-bounded (30 d) and capped (100, newest-first). Older findings stay in `run_events` and remain queryable; they stop being open cards. Both constants are structural with their reasons.
- **The staleness clock no longer resets on its own output (D11).** `LastFindingAt` counted staleness findings as observations, so a stale row would re-propose one horizon later forever instead of staying stale until upstream moved. Replaced by `LastObservedChangeAt`, which excludes them by construction, with the doc comment corrected to match.
- **Live-apply proven for BOTH consumed ⚙ (D12)**, not one: `adoption.price_data_stale_days` now has its own mid-run mutation test alongside `watchlist.fetch_fail_streak`.
- **The `llm_intent` chain corrected (D13)** — see the live-verification bullet above.
- **The S16.4 ten-check walk is enumerated in both new lock entries (D14)**, the promptfoo entry's shape, check by check. #10 stays gate-queued.
- **Array diffs key on identity, not index (D15).** A head insertion shifted every later element, so a one-item addition read as a large run of "changed" keys and raised a false drift finding. Elements carrying `id`/`uuid`/`slug`/`key`/`name` are keyed by it, so an insertion is one added key and a reorder is invisible. Scalar arrays stay positional and say why: a reorder there is genuinely indistinguishable from a change, and over-reporting is the honest direction.
- **Dropped as judged** (recorded so they are not re-litigated): rune-splitting in `clip`/`excerpt` (cosmetic — `json.Marshal` sanitizes), seed coverage against report-02's letter (a closed evidence archive, not the binding contract; the substitutions are documented), and `CadenceMonthly` being unused (legitimate until the §2.1 provider table exists). **Carried to the B5 gate:** `SINET_CDIO_API_KEY` env custody vs R7's broker class — the organ is not installed until that gate, so custody settles with the install.

### §34 drain round 2 (P3-B5-6A, FINAL — the re-check's 4 surviving mediums + 3 smaller items, R1–R7)

Round 1 fixed 13 of its 15 items genuinely. The re-check proved two were fixes in name only, and that the **record itself was wrong in the half the B5-gate proposal rests on**. What changed, and what the earlier record claimed that is not true:

- **R1 — the gzip "belt" was UNREACHABLE and neither half of D1 was behaviourally tested.** `net/http` adds `Accept-Encoding: gzip` itself on every request where the caller set neither that header nor `Range` on a non-HEAD method, so the "unsolicited gzip" stub was transport-negotiated all along (`Uncompressed=true`, header stripped) and the belt's branch never executed. Round 1's "a stub serves real gzip in both shapes" was FALSE — both shapes collapse to one path, and the header deletion was guarded only by a source-grep. The belt is **deleted** (handling what cannot happen through this transport is exactly what the scope rule forbids), the source-scan tripwire on the header stays, and the surviving fix is now locked BEHAVIOURALLY: an origin serving real gzip parses to two entries, and re-adding the hand-set header fails that test on the gzip magic bytes.
- **R2 — the include filters now HOLD real selectors.** Round 1 recorded them as "operator data supplied through the R3 override at bring-up"; that was impossible — `Row` has no filter field and `LoadRows` rejects unknown fields loudly — so `RegionFilters` ignored its argument, `include_filters` (omitempty) never reached the wire, and the test named for region filtering asserted only the subtractive half. The selectors are **code-held per row and verified at build time (2026-07-27)** by fetching every seeded page row with the poller's own User-Agent and reading the served HTML: **14 rows served exactly one `<main>` landmark** and carry `include_filters: ["main"]`; the other 7 served none (kimi.com, synthetic.new, byteplus.com, x.ai, alibabacloud.com — JS shells or bot walls; both help.openai.com articles answered 403, so their landmark belongs to an error page) and carry none, which is a verified fact rather than an omission. **One selector per row deliberately**: the organ CONCATENATES the matches of every include filter (`processors/text_json_diff/processor.py apply_include_filters@0.55.8`), so a nested landmark would duplicate the region rather than widen it. A filter that matches nothing is **loud, never quiet** — the same function raises `FilterNotFoundInResponse`, which the organ records and returns as `last_error` in the watch list Sinet already polls. Asserted on the wire, plus a count check so a key naming no seeded row cannot silently send nothing. No migration, no `Row` field: structural config stays code (the ratified judgment holds *because* the code now holds the data).
- **R3 — the zero-proposal guard gated on the wrong question and swallowed a signal class.** It asked "did we manage to PROPOSE a row?" instead of "did anything IN SCOPE change?". Probed: an upstream that drops in-scope models yields `TotalRows=0, OutOfScope=1403` → the finding was suppressed entirely, **and the row's content hash still advanced, so it was never re-evaluated**. `moved` was built only from providers present upstream, so REMOVALS and models whose price shape stops being flat contributed nothing. The gate is now `HasInScopeChange()`: proposed rows **or** removals **or** a shape change (stopped being one flat rate, or its conditional/tiered numbers moved). Shape comparison is **canonical** — key-order and number-format insensitive — so a regenerated upstream document never manufactures drift. Advancing the hash on a no-in-scope-change pass is safe *because* the diff runs against the STATIC vendored baseline: every byte change that arrives is fully evaluated before that branch is reached. Locked by unit tests for removal, shape change and the out-of-scope negative, plus an end-to-end card.
- **R4 — the "genuinely inexpressible" record was WRONG, and the gate proposal rested on it.** *Not settable over REST* is true and stands (thirteen `add_resource` routes at 0.55.8, none touching application settings). But it **IS settable via environment**, which the earlier grep missed by looking at `flask_app.py` only: `changedetectionio/llm/evaluator.py@0.55.8 get_llm_config` resolves **environment variables FIRST** — verbatim *"Resolution order (first non-empty model wins): 1. Environment variables: LLM_MODEL, LLM_API_KEY, LLM_API_BASE  2. Datastore settings (set via UI)"*, the same three names in that module's docstring — and the datastore fallback is `settings.application.llm.{model,api_key,api_base}` (not `LLMSettings.api_base`; `LLMSettings` is the validation layer `get_llm_settings` hydrates that dict into). So endpoint pointing rides **Sinet's own versioned mechanism**: the generated `sinet-watchlist.service` now carries `Environment=LLM_API_BASE=` (derived from the llama-swap unit's listen address, so moving llama-swap moves the organ with it) and `Environment=LLM_MODEL=openai/<seat model id>` (litellm's custom-endpoint form; **corrected at the round-2 re-check landing, finding F1** — the round-2 text rendered the DUTY ALIAS here, but the generated llama-swap config keys its models map by seat MODEL ID and aliases are swap-invisible (S12.4/R15), so the alias was a name the endpoint beside the unit could never serve; the default is now DERIVED at generation from ⚙ `local.alias[watchlist-triage]` → manifest seat (default: fast → `Qwen3.5-4B`), cross-checked by test against the generated llama-swap config's own keys, with the `Params` override kept for a non-Sinet endpoint). `Environment=LLM_API_KEY=sk-local-no-auth` is also rendered (**corrected at the same landing, finding F2** — the round-2 text claimed no key is needed, true of the SERVER but not the client chain: `llm/client.py@0.55.8` forwards `api_key` only when non-empty, litellm falls back to `OPENAI_API_KEY` — absent in a unit env — and hands `None` to the OpenAI SDK, whose constructor raises before any request; the value is a FIXED NON-SECRET placeholder, not a credential, so S11.5 stays satisfied). The honest statement replacing round 1's "an operator install-time step": **not settable over REST, but settable via env on the unit** — installing and starting that unit is the operator act, the configuration itself is code. The test-only `LocalEndpointConfig` / `RequiredLocalEndpointConfig` helper is **deleted**; the generated unit is the production consumer.
- **R5 — two suites asserted DOC PROSE in the file under review** (§10: suites assert behaviour, never documentation), and one asserted a tautology (`RequiredLocalEndpointConfig` returning its own arguments). Both are gone: the Miniflux test keeps only its real negative (no HMAC receiver in the file the fallback would replace — the in-repo tripwire precedent), and the endpoint test is replaced by the unit-generation behaviour above.
- **R6 — the identity-keyed array diff's limitation is now stated.** Duplicate identity values inside one array collapse to a single leaf path (later overwrites earlier), so a change confined to a masked element is invisible — the one place this diff can UNDER-report. `name` is the weakest and likeliest to repeat, which is why it is last in the preference list; a compound or occurrence-suffixed key would reintroduce the positional cascade the keying exists to remove, so the trade is recorded, not papered over.
- **R7 — the D4 scale check was structurally blind to a D2-class mis-hint**: it forced `ParserHint="atom"` on every feed row. It now keeps each row's **seeded** hint and serves the dialect the ORIGIN actually serves (live-verified 2026-07-27 by root element: `github.com/*.atom` → `<feed>`, `openrouter.ai/blog/feed.xml` → `<rss>`, `hnrss.org` → `<rss>`, `reddit.com/…/.rss` → `<feed>`, the D2 trap), and asserts **zero parse failures**; a seeded origin whose dialect has never been verified fails the test rather than being assumed.
- **Also corrected:** `cdio.go`'s `llm_intent` comment still read "resolved watch → tag → global" although drain r1 had already corrected that claim in this section — aligned to the verified chain (watch → the first tag carrying an intent → `('', '')`, no global fallback, no evaluation at all).

### §34-B — the API canary layer (P3-B5-6B; S14.6 ¶3, S03.6–S03.7, S10.1/S10.5, S14.2, S14.4)

The B half of the A/B split, taken at the event-type seam: A owns `drift.finding`, **B owns `canary.result`**. Where the executor watches what providers SAY about themselves (pages, feeds, aggregator APIs), the canary layer watches what they actually DO on the wire, per lane. Same package, same import wall, same "package owns the LOGIC, shell owns WHEN" posture; **no new ⚙, no new migration, no new Go module, no new `components.lock` entry** (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged at 118 keys / 33 domains; migrations stay `0001`–`0013`; lock stays 21; `go.mod` unchanged).

- **Package map:** `canary.go` (the layer, the ⚙ seam, dueness, the record+card path), `canary_auth.go`, `canary_behavioral.go`, `canary_logprob.go`, `canary_models.go`, `canary_conformance.go`. Composition is `internal/shell/watchlist_seams.go` (`buildCanaryLayer` + `canaryLoop`). **Import wall extended by exactly two edges** — `internal/scheduler` (the S10.5 classifier this layer is the first production caller of) and `internal/conformance` (the S14.5 registry whose adapter-suite dueness it surfaces and whose `RecordResult` verb it records through). Both are leaves on this package's side of the graph, so the wall's REASON — keeping `evals→verify→intake→adapters` out of an observability leaf — still holds. **`internal/evals` stays BARRED**: the behavioral canary rides a func seam composed at the shell root exactly as 6A's `Revalidate` does, with `BehavioralOutcome` a mirror of the runner outcome (the §33 `local.SuiteResult` no-import-either-way precedent). `internal/settings` stays barred; the layer's own `CanarySettings` adds `Float`/`Duration` so `scheduler.LoadLimitConfig` is satisfied structurally without an import.
- **`canary.result` MINTED** (the §29 flip: real provenance in `contract.go` + a `producerTypes()` entry in the same packet). Inventory **82 minted / 11 declare-only** (93 registered) — **family 11, the seam the split was taken at, is now fully minted**. Platform-scope (`UserID: "platform"`, no `RunID` — a canary run has no run), `SchemaVersion: 1`, verdict `CONFORMS`. The payload's first four fields are the `FamilyDriftCanary` contract minimum's canary half **verbatim**: `canary_kind` + `lane` + `result` (pass|fail) + `delta`; it joins the §29 required-field golden set. `internal/api/topics.go` needed **no edit** again — the existing family map routes `canary.result` to `inbox`, proven by test.
- **Cards derive from ONE query.** A FAILING canary raises its card as a **`drift.finding`** through 6A's existing emitter, so `InboxSnapshot.DriftCards`, the fingerprint-per-incident-window dedup, the horizon bound and the S14.8 revalidation edge all apply unchanged — **`internal/api/projection.go`, `topics.go` and `sse.go` are byte-unchanged by this packet** (a checkable negative, asserted by a test that a two-subject canary run folds into one operator-only card). `canary.result` is the per-run RECORD; the card is the drift finding. A passing canary and a first-observation baseline raise nothing.
- **Auth canary (R23) — `scheduler.Classify`'s first production caller.** One cheap real request per paid lane on ⚙ `canary.auth_interval`, normalized into `scheduler.LimitSignal` **including `OnValidCredentials`** (true because the canary probes with the lane's KNOWN-GOOD stored credential — which is exactly what makes a 403 here mean policy revocation rather than a stale key, P-T17-1), then handed to `Classify`. **The classifier is not re-implemented, forked or bypassed**: a source scan asserts none of the Z.AI wire codes (1302/1305/1308/1310/1113) appears in this package, and that `scheduler.Classify(` is actually called.
