Locked by a verify-leg test whose fixture is deliberately bare — there is no deliverable to verify, so parked-and-clean is only reachable if the boundary actually fired — plus a structural scan pinning all four boundaries, so a leg added later without one is caught.
- **D5 — a budget could be declared for nobody.** `budgets` carries no foreign key (the `lanes` precedent), so an operator typo minted a ghost row that the recreated view then served as a real declaration. `Declare` now makes the same refusal `SetPause` already made, and both share one `ErrNoSuchPerson` sentinel. Tested: the refusal, that NO row lands, the same refusal on the other switch, and that a real person still works.
- **D6 — that refusal surfaced as a 500.** `person` is client input, so whether it names somebody is answered at the BOUNDARY now (`requirePerson`) and answered 404 — the part-A drain-D7 principle, that a caller's mistake must not read as a platform fault. Both switches tested for the 404, for nothing written, for nothing audited, and for the real-person direction still working.
- **D7 — the never-percent scan missed three shapes.** Extended over `FlagSuppressed` / `DriftDismissed` / `ConformanceAcknowledged` by driving the three card verbs inside the scan's own fixture.
- **D8 — an overstated claim.** "byte-for-byte the behavior it pins" is wrong about the TEXT: 0016's absence reason said the platform persists no budget at all, and that sentence is now false. Reworded to say what is actually true — every property the landed test pins still holds for an undeclared person; the reason string differs, and it had to.

### §39-C — the BENCH-REG driver, the `.direct` leg & the verdict backend (P3-B6-2C; S14.7, BENCH-REG §2/§3/§4/§12/§14, S15.6)

The **third and last part** of B6-2. Parts A and B above are unchanged: this subsection records only what part C decided. Part C closes the one gap the whole practice was resting on — **no production code called `DispatchDirectArm`/`RenderBlind`, nothing executed a direct arm, and `benchmarkDirectTextSeam` was an honest refusal**, so every sampled pair rested at `sampled` forever (§35 D4 named this as its own obligation). Part C opens **migration 0018** — the packet's own, immutable from landing; 0001–0017 are byte-untouched and `user_version` moves 17 → 18.

- **The protocol is still BENCH-REG, and part C restates NONE of it.** `registered.go` and `Spec/benchmark-preregistration-v1.md` are byte-unchanged. The transport passes the caller's verdict, guess and §12 disposition strings THROUGH to the package, which checks them against the registration; `internal/api` carries no gate limb, no threshold, no label, no domain name and no disposition vocabulary, asserted by a source scan over every non-test file with its own planted-value probe. `internal/api` imports `internal/benchmark` in neither direction (the reverse wall) — the verdict backend rides a narrow `BenchmarkSurface` seam whose reads are the package's own shapes marshaled verbatim, so the blindness property `PendingPair` carries travels to the wire intact rather than being re-declared.
- **Migration 0018 is ONE column and its NULL is load-bearing.** `benchmark_pairs.direct_text`, nullable: `NULL` = no capture (the honest absence that keeps a pair unrendered), `''` = the arm ran and produced nothing (a real single-shot outcome, "take what comes"), text = the answer verbatim, never truncated by the platform (§3.2 reports the length confound and never corrects it). A `DEFAULT ''` would have collapsed the first two and made every never-dispatched pair look like an arm that answered with silence. The home is the pair row on this table's own `render_a`/`render_b` precedent — small text artifacts OF the practice, inside the S02.9 durable set and every S13.10 archive by construction — and it is **never an event body**: the §14 record carries refs, and a model's full answer is exactly the bulky payload ⚙ `state.event_payload_cap` exists to keep out of the log (asserted by a planted-marker scan of `run_events`).
- **OQ9 — the leg drives the ADAPTER path, and `internal/stage` gains no benchmark import.** `Skeleton.Dispatch` learns the `.direct` role and runs ONE session through `Driver.Drive`, so the run completes under the ordinary FSM (claimed→running→terminal) with its D7 checkpoint per paid call and its metering intact — the §14 record needs that consumption, and an unmetered arm makes the §13 done-directly figure unanswerable. It deliberately does NOT go through `Skeleton.Session`, whose budget watcher exists to propose a stage split: a split is a successor session on a consolidated ledger, which is precisely the multi-turn behaviour §2 forbids. The two things the package must not know cross as func seams composed at the shell root (`Config.BenchmarkStatement` / `BenchmarkCapture`, the Snapshot/LedgerRevision/Paused precedent), late-bound like `pseams.pipe`; the run-id suffix and the dispatch role are pinned against each other at boot (`checkBenchmarkDirectSuffix`, the `checkBenchmarkDomainMapping` precedent), because a `.direct` run no role matches would leave every dispatched pair resting forever.
- **The prompt is the frozen statement ALONE, asserted field by field.** No assembled brief, no pinned-context file, no knowledge slice, no compiled worker, no agent definition, no tool allowlist, no gated tools, no permission mode, no recitation valve, no `OnEvent` watcher and no `OnSession` registration. "The prompt looks right" is not the claim — the claim is that nothing the platform adds reached the arm, so the test reads the recorded `StartRequest` member by member and a source scan of `direct.go` refuses the constructs that would make the arm multi-turn (`Session(`, `newBudgetWatcher`, `splitRequester`, `newReciter`, `DriveResume`, `Assemble`, `CompileForRun`, `launchRole`, `cancels.register`, `pauseParkPoint`, `internal/benchmark`) with a non-tautology probe. **No `stage.started`/`stage.finished` is minted**: §38 OQ3(a) scopes the stage boundary to `Skeleton.Session`, and this is the run's own single session, whose record is the run-lifecycle rows.
- **⚠ A DECLARED READING, reported rather than papered over — the direct arm runs TOOL-LESS.** §6 (frozen, registered) says "the direct arm's surface-native tools (e.g., its own web search) are allowed as-is — the baseline is *what the requester's subscription actually does*, not a stripped model". This lane cannot express that: `--tools` is always lowered from `Worker.ToolAllowlist`, so ANY list the platform composes is platform shaping (a worker template by another name, which the disposition forbids), and an empty list is the only value the platform does not author. It is also the only shape whose output the capture can see — the arm's run has its own empty scratch cwd, so a tool-written file would go nowhere the §14 record could point at. So the leg passes no toolset, and the gap is recorded here as **parity material beyond the §6 declaration**, which §6 itself says is "new registration material, never silently absorbed" (BENCH-REG §17 governs the fix; a §6 amendment or an adapter change is the coordinator's call, not the executor's). **This is a referral line for the coordinator, not an acceptance.** The same lane constraint forces a model to be named at all (S03.2 makes it a per-invocation channel and the lowering refuses an empty one), so the platform passes its own EXECUTION duty seat and the §14 record carries the identity metering OBSERVED — which is the figure §6 says is authoritative, since the direct arm's identity "cannot be pinned or controlled".
- **TAKE WHAT COMES — but only from an arm that actually RAN (corrected at drain r1).** A session that ended on its own terms, or that an ordinary ceiling cut short, produced something (possibly nothing at all, which is a real single-shot outcome): its text is captured verbatim, the pair is rendered and voted on, and the landed §6 parity note records at verdict time that the run did not end cleanly. A **CRASH is different in kind and is NOT captured**: an engine that died — or never spawned — did not answer the frozen statement at all, so an empty capture would render "the platform versus a platform failure" and ask a person to vote on it. The capture stays absent, and the pair becomes the failed-pair card below. Nothing re-runs, nothing resumes, nothing retries on any of these paths (asserted: one `Start`, zero `Resume`, on both a died-at-gate and a spawn-crash fixture). A failed CAPTURE is loud and does not fail the run — the run's disposition already committed and the pair waits rather than advancing on a text nobody stored.
- **R18 — the driver has NO PROTOCOL AUTHORITY OF ITS OWN.** The landed 1-min benchmark tick gains a second half: list `sampled` pairs and ask `DispatchDirectArm`, list `dispatched` pairs whose direct run has ENDED and ask `RenderBlind`. Whether a pair may advance stays entirely the package's (both verbs check state; the runs-table PK means **no second arm RUN can exist** — corrected at drain r1: `DispatchDirectArm` deliberately TOLERATES `run.ErrExists`, which is landed B5-7 crash-resume idempotency, so the PK does not refuse the CALL, it refuses a second row), and the pass writes no state at all. Dueness is the STORED state — the tick decides only how promptly a card appears — and a failure is logged and retried on the NEXT pass by re-reading state, which is safe re-entry precisely because the verbs check state themselves. **"Ended" is `run.IsTerminal`, and `crashed` is deliberately not that**: S02.3/S02.5 make recovery the owner of a crashed run's disposition (harvest, fork, tombstone, finalize), so a crashed arm waits for that decision instead of being rendered out from under it. `benchmarkDriverBatch` = 32 is structural with its reason (one pass shares the single writer connection, S02.1; the bound decides how many pairs a pass picks up, never which are due, and the listing is oldest-draw-first so a skipped pair is the next pass's first item), as is `stateBatchCap` = 256 on the read itself.
- **R20 — the verdict backend, and the two orderings it exists to hold.** `GET /api/benchmark/verdicts` serves the package's pre-record shape (a member their own, the operator all — the landed inbox derivation's own scope). `POST /api/approvals/{id}/verdict` calls `RecordVerdict` FIRST and `Reveal` only after it returns, so the §3.4 order is a property of the handler and is asserted on the CALL ORDER rather than on a response that would look identical either way; the end-to-end in `internal/shell` proves the record row and the `benchmark.pair_recorded` event both exist before the reveal read, and that a reveal asked for beforehand fails. The MANDATORY guess stays structural: `NewAnswer` is called in the shell adapter, its two failure modes are both malformed arguments (400, with `guess_required` as its own machine code), and nothing is recorded when one is attempted. `POST …/decline` → `Decline`, `POST …/dispose` → `DisposeAlarm`, `POST /api/benchmark/opt-in` → `SetOptIn`.
- **A verdict is the REQUESTER's and is not delegable — the operator is inside that refusal.** They asked for the work, they read both answers, and the vote is their judgement of their own deliverable; an administrator voting on somebody else's pair would put a judgement in the record under a name that never saw the task. So the role bit decides VISIBILITY (the operator reads every pending card) and never authorship (403 on the vote and on the decline) — the same line part A drew for asks under D10. OQ10's `SetOptIn` is the same shape one step further: consent is refused for anybody but the actor **at the transport AND inside the verb**, and neither statement is the only one.
- **§29 — no new type, no new producer, and NOTHING minted at the transport.** Every part-C act already carries a landed canonical row: a verdict and a decline write the §14 `benchmark.pair_recorded`, and `DisposeAlarm`/`SetOptIn` mint their own family-5 `decision.recorded` INSIDE their own transactions. So `internal/api` mints nothing here (OQ8) — a second row would make one decision look like two — asserted by driving every new route and requiring the family-5 count to be unchanged, with the call count proving the walk was not vacuous. Inventory stays **90 minted / 5 declare-only / 95 registered**.
- **The redaction line moved for one shape, and the reason is measurement, not taste.** `redactionEdgeFiles` stays at **three** — the reveal is payload-derived and goes out through the shared `writeReadRedacted` (the `meters.go`/`historyapi.go` precedent, so the primitive does not appear in `benchmark.go`), while the **blind render bodies go out UNWRAPPED**. They are S13/S06 product content off the pair row (§7-C2·2, §38 ruling (b)), and beyond that standing rule a redactor between the two arms would be a HEURISTIC inside the comparison: a false positive firing on one side and not the other changes what the voter compared, which on this one surface is a measurement artifact rather than a cosmetic one (§3.2 — the template is uniform and the platform never edits an arm).
- **The two benchmark cards joined the D9 agreement.** Parts A/B listed them `answerable:false` with a reason naming part C; they now compute `answerable` per CALLER from the same predicates their verbs enforce (`mayAnswerVerdict` requester-only, `mayDisposeAlarm` operator-only) and name their live routes in `actions`. The two rules point in OPPOSITE directions, so both are asserted. Neither runs through `applyTierRules`: a verdict is never batched (the arm-guess is per pair and structural) and neither demands a PIN, and a card claiming a door that does not exist is the defect D9 exists to prevent.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged, **118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), and `registered.go`, `Spec/benchmark-preregistration-v1.md`, `internal/history`, `internal/redact`, `internal/watchlist`, `internal/conformance`, `internal/watchdog` and migrations 0001–0017 are untouched. The §35 guardrails stay green: the benchmark import wall (part C adds only stdlib-shaped reads), the no-kill/set-nothing wall (nothing new names a kill, park or transition ident), the reverse api wall, and part A's tree-wide `TestCancelIsReachableFromTheHTTPVerbsOnly`, which still allows exactly three files — the driver and the leg reach no cancel entry point. **$0** — every arm in the battery is a tier-F fake, real accrual is a bring-up act under BENCH-REG §4.1's own schedule, the opt-in default stays OFF, and no test flips anyone's opt-in except through `SetOptIn`'s own self-only rule. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.

#### §39-C drain round 1 (P3-B6-2C drain r1, D1–D6) — corrections

The fresh-context evaluation returned FAIL on 1 MAJOR plus 1 low and 3 nits, having verified the rest clean — including the tool-less ruling's basis at the lowering and the full end-to-end. The MAJOR was a real protocol breach on a path the packet's own tests had proven ROUTABLE without asking what happened next. All 6 items applied; two §39-C claims above are corrected IN PLACE (a record correction of an error, not a dated-truth append) and are marked as such.

- **D1 — a crashed `.direct` run took the recovery ladder's DEFAULT FORK, and the fork re-entered the leg.** The packet's own `runRole` test proved `bp-7.direct.g1` routes to `roleDirect`, so the S02.5 step-3 default would have produced a SECOND billed engine run on the same frozen §2 statement — whose output the capture could never have taken anyway, because it keys the parent run id. That is not a recovery, it is a protocol breach that also spends the requester's own budget to produce nothing. **Coordinator disposition: BENCH-REG §2's single-shot contract OUTRANKS the S02.5 default for this run class.** `recovery.Config` gains a `NoFork func(runID string) bool` PREDICATE SEAM — `internal/recovery` learns nothing about benchmarks, and nil is the pre-drain behavior exactly — and the shell composes it with `stage.IsDirectArmRun`, the SAME function `Skeleton.Dispatch` routes on, so "which runs are single-shot" has one home and the router and the fork-refusal cannot disagree. The disposition becomes crashed→`finalized` (a ratified S02.3 edge) with its per-edge citation in the transition detail; no generation bump, because nothing was fenced. The crashed arm's partial spend is already on the ledger and the checkpoints, so nothing is lost by not re-running it. Locked by a ONE-fixture, one-pass test where the direct arm finalizes and an ordinary crashed run beside it still FORKS (the predicate must not over-match), plus a **revert probe**: with the predicate nil the old fork returns and the assertion fails.
- **D2 — the stranded pair is now HUMAN-VISIBLE and CLOSEABLE.** With D1 the arm ends terminal with no capture, and the pre-drain driver would have retried `RenderBlind` every minute forever while no surface showed the requester anything. Three things changed. **(a)** The driver SKIPS a dispatched pair whose arm has ended with no capture — state-derived and silent, not a logged failure, because the state will not change. **(b)** An EIGHTH derived inbox kind, `BenchmarkFailedPairs`: pair not yet terminal + its direct-arm run terminal + capture NULL, REQUESTER-owned exactly as the verdict card is (the operator sees, the requester acts). It is a pure derivation over rows that already exist — **no side store, no new pair state, no new event type, no schema change** — carrying the arm's own FSM state and the honest one-line story, with ONE action: the landed decline. Its card id shares the verdict-card space because it is the same pair and the same verb closes it; tier Low, because nothing is at risk and nothing is blocked. `crashed` is excluded on purpose (S02.5 makes recovery the owner of a crashed run's disposition, so that pair is still being decided) — the same reading the driver's own wait takes, and the api's terminal-state mirror is cross-checked against `run.IsTerminal` by test so a later FSM edge fails loudly here. **(c)** The §14 decline record closes it and **the registered §14 schema is NOT extended**: the record already names the direct run, and the crash CAUSE stays derivable by joining that run id to the run's own state — a recorded reading, and the reason no field was added to signed text. **(d)** Crash-declines and consent-declines both land in the §4.2.5 decline rate and are not separated; that separation is flagged to the SAME named bring-up consideration as the tool-less arm, since both are §6/§17 registration material rather than code decisions. Locked end to end on the fake engine: arm crashes → no fork → parent finalized → the card appears for its requester and not for another member (whose own stranded pair appears for THEM — the non-tautological direction) → operator sees it un-answerable → decline → pair closed with exactly one §14 record → the driver never visits it again.
- **D3 — the runs-PK claim was overstated** and is corrected in place above: `DispatchDirectArm` tolerates `run.ErrExists` (landed B5-7 crash-resume idempotency), so the true property is that no second arm RUN can exist. The evaluator's own hostile probe is now a permanent test: flip a dispatched pair back to `sampled` by SQL, re-dispatch and re-claim, and the walk still ends with one run row, one queue row and ONE engine execution.
- **D4 — a counter test claimed a pin it did not make.** `TestPartCCountersArePinned`'s comment said it held the adoption lock at 21 while the body asserted nothing of the kind. The comment now says why it deliberately does not: `go run ./tools/lockgate` is the real gate and checks coverage both ways, and a hand-counted 21 here would be a second, weaker authority that could pass while the real gate failed.
- **D5 — a carried fact was imprecise, and the LANDED derivation is what is correct.** The brief's "a member-owned card the operator does not see" reads as a visibility rule; it is not one. The operator SEES every rendered pair (S01.9, and the landed inbox derivation does exactly this); what is requester-only is AUTHORSHIP — the vote, the decline and the consent flip, with the operator refused 403 on each. §35's ownership sentence is about CONSENT, not visibility, and the two are different properties of the same card.
- **D6 — a wrong comment.** "Another member cannot even see it exists" misdescribed a 403: the pair exists, so the house 404-for-missing / 403-for-another-owner shape applies. Their READ is what is scoped, which the assertion immediately below it already proves.

## 40. The S15.9 settings family — registry surface, UISchema emitter & the price table (P3-B6-3A; S15.9, S01.10, S18.1/S18.3/S18.4, S10.3, S15.2)

The **first of three parts** of B6-3 (the coordinator split A/B/C, 2026-07-28). Part A is the S15.9 settings family: the one registry read (the two generated artifacts + values + effective clamp bounds + the S18.4 R9 registered block), the per-setting audit history, the validated write verbs, and the S18.3 price table with its migration and read-through. **Parts B (`/api/deliverables` + accept + preview) and C (`/api/memory`) are LATER packets — their seams stay held-not-routed (`Config.Accept`, `Config.Preview`) and nothing of them is built.** Part A opens **migration 0019** — the packet's own, immutable from landing; 0001–0018 are byte-untouched and `user_version` moves 18 → 19.

- **The UISchema emitter is the REGISTRY's, and it emits no rule.** S15.9 binds the UI to "the registry-emitted JSON Schema + UISchema (S01.10)", and S01.10 (b) is the reason it cannot be an api-layer improvisation: one artifact set drives validation, the UI and the docs so they cannot drift. `UISchema()` is a sibling of `JSONSchema()` in `internal/settings` (index.go byte-unchanged, the declarations never move), emitting FC-v1 §4's core vocabulary — a `Categorization` whose 33 `Category` tabs are the S18 domains in declared order, one `Control` per key, and a `Group` exactly where the key index itself declares a sub-namespace **shared by two or more keys** (`local.ttl.*`, `memory.injection_budget_tokens.*`, `intake.clearance_floor.*`, `memory.vector_gate.*` — four of them). A lone deep key sits directly in its Category, because a Group of one is a box drawn around nothing. **v0 emits NO SHOW/HIDE rule** and the reason is recorded in code and in a scan with its own probe: the registry holds no datum saying a setting is meaningful only under a condition on another. `restartRequired`/`auto`/`perUser`/`dormant` are BADGES a person reads, and the S18.2 relational clamps are validated on write by the registry, never by hiding a control — fabricating a rule to exercise the vocabulary is what the honest-absence discipline forbids. The capability is present structurally; a later packet with a real condition adds a `rule` member and nothing else moves.
- **Completeness is pinned against the INDEX, not against a golden.** Every one of the 118 keys has exactly one Control; every Control's `scope` resolves as a JSON Pointer into the emitted schema AND lands on a leaf carrying `x-sinet` (a control bound to a group node would edit an object); two emissions are byte-identical; and the badges on each Control are asserted to AGREE with that leaf's `x-sinet` in both directions — one truth with two readers, rather than two truths. A key added to index.go later without a control fails the build. The Category label is built exactly as schema.go builds the domain node's title, and `TestEveryDomainHasOneOwningSection` pins the S18.4 R10 fact that label rests on.
- **OQ8 — settings writes are operator-only, and that is STRUCTURAL rather than a policy this transport chose.** `settings.Actor.validate` admits exactly `operator` and `automation`; there is no member kind a write could be attributed to, and S18.1 calls the index operator-editable. So a member cannot edit even a per-user key's own override — the operator administers it via `for_user` — and the transport constructs an operator actor and only an operator actor, which is what keeps automation's G1 rider 1 split (values within bounds; only the operator moves bounds) uncrossable from HTTP. The **dev identity is refused too**, on its own separate limb: §9's dev-posture fallback reads and never administers, and it reads as operator-equivalent, so a role-bit check alone would have let a login-less dev process rewrite the platform's configuration. Every refusal states which of the two conditions failed.
- **READS are session-required and available to everybody; only the per-user overrides are owner-scoped.** The declarations, defaults, effective bounds and help are the platform's own configuration and not anybody's data, so a member reads the whole index (the non-tautological direction: 118 values, not an empty answer) — but a per-user override IS about a person, so a member sees only their own and the operator sees all (§30's shape applied to the one per-person member of the response). The read also STATES the caller's own authority (`editable` + its reason), so a surface renders a read-only view instead of controls that will 403.
- **The write verbs add no semantics — they route the landed ones.** Set/reset is `Registry.Set` (`value:null` = reset, which DELETES the override row, so reset needs no verb of its own and cannot drift from what the registry means by it); bounds is `Registry.SetBounds`. The clamp, the type/enum check and the S18.2 relational rules refuse with the REGISTRY's own message, mapped to 400/404/503 — a message this transport paraphrased would be a second, worse explanation. Every write already lands {override cell + `settings_events` row + `settings.changed`} in ONE transaction, so **nothing is co-minted**: an act with a landed canonical row is never double-minted (§39 OQ8), asserted by a walk over set/bounds/reset that counts 3 events, 3 audit rows and **zero `decision.recorded`**, and by the negative that a refused write leaves neither.
- **⚠ OQ1's audit branch: `settings.changed` does NOT fit, so `price.row_added` is REGISTERED — the report branch, taken deliberately.** The disposition asked the executor to ground the contract and take the honest branch. `settings.changed`'s contract minimum is `{actor, key, old, new, reason}` **mirroring `settings_events`** (the frozen S14.2 family-12 descriptor says so in those words): `key` is a DECLARED dotted registry key, and old→new is a value REPLACEMENT. A price row has neither — it is identified by `{model, lane, effective_from}`, and an effective-dated append leaves every earlier row in force for the dates it covers, so nothing is replaced. Reusing the type would have needed a pseudo-key and a fabricated `old`: two false fields in the audit trail to avoid registering one honest type, and the pseudo-key is the exact stretch the disposition banned for `settings_events`. So `price.row_added` lands under §29 same-packet mechanics — contract entry + `producerTypes()` entry, `ADMIT` into family 12 under the same OQ2-(A) broadened-Platform reading that homes `registry.*`/`preview.*`/`local.*`/`history.*`, no new family and therefore no S00.9 amendment. Its own contract minimum rides its TypeSpec Note (the `history.query_audited` precedent — the family descriptor is frozen spec text this packet may not author), with a required-field golden. Inventory **90 minted / 5 declare-only / 95 → 91 / 5 / 96**, and the payload is asserted to carry NEITHER a `key` NOR an `old`.
- **Migration 0019 `price_rows` is one table, append-only, shipped EMPTY.** S01.10 said the shape: "the D5 price table is its own owner-attributed table under the same audit pattern". Columns are the S10.3 row `{model, lane, unit_prices JSON, effective_from, verified_on, source}` plus `created_by`/`created_ts`/`reason`. **Append-only is the EDIT MODEL, not a restriction on it**: a change is a new effective-dated row, so the row that priced a past receipt stays exactly as it was and a receipt stays reproducible — the two blanket triggers make that structural (the 0007 `review_comment_anchors` / 0012 `revalidation_stamps` precedent), probed with raw SQL on three UPDATE column groups plus a DELETE — four probes in all. (This sentence originally read "four column groups plus DELETE", which miscounted the probes; corrected in place at drain r1 — a record correction of an error, not a dated-truth append.) **Nothing seeds it**: an empty table prices every row UNPRICED, never $0 (S10.1; P-T08-1), the B1-gate posture stands, and filling it is an operator act this surface now makes possible. NO MONEY IS COMPUTED IN THE MIGRATION — `unit_prices` is opaque JSON with no view, expression or generated column over it.
- **The read-through is what makes an edit live, and the digest is what makes it visible.** `LivePriceTable` composes an `EffectiveDatedTable` from the stored rows at boot and swaps a freshly built one in under a lock on every append, so a price edit is in force for the next priced call with no restart and no reload call by the caller. Its `Version()` is `PriceRowsDigest` — sha256 over the sorted `{model, lane, effective_from, source, rates}` lines, which makes it a function of the row SET rather than of read order or row ids (asserted both ways: a moved RATE moves it, a changed row id does not). An empty set keeps the landed `empty-v0` label, so the shipped posture is byte-identical to what the platform already had.
- **The digest reaches the S02.6 fingerprint, and the never-faked rule is unchanged.** `projectSeams.Fingerprint` now fills `PriceTableVersion` — S10.3 makes price drift a first-class staleness cause ("it re-prices the remaining plan, so any change fires the freshness re-validation trigger", G1 Def.5) and until 0019 that member was a fixed label, so filling it would have been fabricating a constant. It rides even when no project matched, because a price is not a property of a project. **The landed `TestProjectSeamsFingerprint` guard passes UNCHANGED**: with no table composed the member stays empty, which is still exactly what "unobservable members stay empty" means; the WITH-table direction is asserted in the new shell battery, which also drives the landed `EvaluateFreshness` and requires it to report price-table drift — with an unmoved-table control, so the trigger is not simply always-on.
- **⚠ Two S18.3 surfaces are DEFERRED with their reason SERVED AS DATA, per OQ1.** The per-model flat/metered flag (S10.2) and the `overflow_mode`/`region_model_gate` pair (S03.6) do not ship, and the price read carries both absences with the reason attached: their consumer is ratified EMPTY at v0 (`MeteredExceptions`, G1 P7), and **a flag with no consumer fakes a capability** — a stored setting nothing reads is a dial connected to nothing. Deferral is served, not merely commented, so the surface says what it does not do.
- **`internal/api` never reaches the pricing layer, and the row is opaque to it in both directions.** The §38 R8 money scan bans the metering import and the price-table identifier outright, so the row crosses `api.PriceSurface` as JSON: S10 owns what a row is and validates it, `internal/shell/price_seams.go` is the ONE place the two representations meet, and it refuses unknown members (`DisallowUnknownFields`) because a misspelled rate would otherwise store silently as zero — the exact figure S10.3's coverage guard exists to keep off a receipt. A bad body is a 400 naming the field (the §39-B drain-D6 principle), never a 500. The response type is named `StoredPricesView` for the same scan's reason.
- **Refresh-as-proposal is re-asserted over the new surface.** An AST scan over every non-test file in the tree allows `AddRow` in exactly two places — the machinery and the operator verb's adapter — with a vacuity guard, plus a source scan proving `internal/watchlist` names neither the store nor the append. B5-6A still PROPOSES drift cards and never applies one (G2 Def.16; §34), and `internal/watchlist` is byte-untouched.
- **The mechanical lockstep companions moved with 0019, and there were SIX, not the two the brief named.** `internal/evals` and `internal/conformance` were the named `user_version == 18` pins; grounding found two more (`internal/api/meters_verbs_test.go`, `internal/api/benchmark_test.go`) plus a **filename sentinel** in the latter (`> "0018_zzz"`), and the preview never-adds-a-migration guard turned out to be a `>= "0019"` string sentinel rather than a list literal. All six moved with their comment chains extended. **⚠ One touch outside the brief's untouched list, declared:** `internal/history/importwall_test.go` holds a THIRD registry-size pin (95 → 96) that the OQ1 report branch necessarily moves — `git diff internal/history/` is one count assertion and its comment, and the package's guardrail, audit and confidence logic is byte-untouched, the §39-B one-sanctioned-line precedent. This is a referral line for the coordinator, not an acceptance.
- **`BenchmarkSurface` gains one additive read for the S18.4 R9 block, and no registered number enters `internal/api`.** R9 says the frozen values "surface in the registry marked 'registered — changing this value requires a re-registration commit'"; the marker travels ON each value from the package that owns them, through the sanctioned seam (`internal/api` imports `internal/benchmark` in neither direction, §39-C), so a display can never disagree with the machinery. A nil practice is an honest absence WITH its reason, never an empty list reading as "nothing is registered". Engine pins are NOT served here — R9's own words give them the manifest home, `components.lock`. A source scan with a probe asserts no registered literal in the package.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` **byte-unchanged, 118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), and `internal/redact`, `internal/benchmark` (incl. `registered.go`), `internal/watchdog`, `internal/watchlist` and `internal/history`'s own logic are untouched. `redactionEdgeFiles` stays at **three**: settings values, audit rows and price rows are CONTENT served UNWRAPPED (§38 ruling (b)) — they are read from their OWN tables, not lifted from a `run_events` payload — so the edge does not widen and the enumeration stays honest. New bounds are structural with named reasons: `settingsHistoryCap` = `readPageDefault` (one read surface, one page size). Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. The never-percent scan is extended over every new shape, and the route table carries no update/delete verb for a price row and no `/v1`. **$0** — every test is hermetic and tier-F; nothing spawns a process, dials a network or prices a real call.

### §40-B — `/api/deliverables`, the accept and the preview verbs (P3-B6-3B; S13.1–S13.4, S13.6, S13.8, S15.2, S15.8, FC-v1 §2)

The **second of three parts** of B6-3. Part A's text above is unchanged: this subsection records only what part B decided. Part B is the S13 content family — the owner-scoped deliverable list and detail with its doors, the per-type comparison data, the anchored-comment surface, the High-tier accept, and the S13.8 preview verbs. **Part C (`/api/memory`) is a later packet; nothing of it is built.** Part B opens **NO migration** (revisions, comments and their placements all landed in 0007; previews have no durable table by design, §25) — 0001–0019 are byte-untouched and `user_version` stays 19 — and **NO new event type**: every act it adds has a landed canonical row, so the inventory stays **91 minted / 5 declare-only / 96 registered**.

- **The review store is a narrow HELD dependency, not a new interface.** `api.Config.Review *review.Store` follows the `*gates.Journal` / `*accept.Accepter` / `*settings.Registry` precedent (§39): `internal/review` is a leaf over storage+eventlog, so the dependency is acyclic and narrow, and an interface would only be a second name for one type. Everything except the LIST goes through its verbs — `Revisions`, `Compare`, `PlacedComments`, `AddComment` — because S13 owns what a revision is, what two of them compare to, and where a comment anchors after a port. The list is the one transport-owned read (bounded, filtered, newest-first) because the store has no list verb and a page is a transport concern; it is nonetheless gated on the review store being wired, on the **one family, one readiness** rule — a list whose every row's detail link answers 503 is worse than saying the family is not composed.
- **OQ2 — "comment CRUD" ships Create + Read, and the absence is a TEST.** Migration 0007 makes a comment immutable ("only the one-way consumption stamp moves") and never deleted (P-T12-2), and THE drain is its one consumer. So no update verb and no delete verb are routed, at any shape one might be reached through (PUT/PATCH/DELETE on the collection and on a per-comment path, plus POST-as-edit — seven probes), each asserted not to answer 200, with the read verb as the non-tautological control and the landed triggers re-probed underneath through raw SQL. "Own comments" is authorship attribution — every comment carries its author — never a mutation right.
- **OQ3 — doors-as-data, and the door agrees with the act in BOTH directions.** The detail names, per deliverable state, the LIVE route with its preconditions: accept (open only when in-review AND repo-backed, naming where its `payload_hash` comes from), request-revision (parked on a rework card ⇒ the open ask id + the `revise_with_guidance` answer route + **the card's own `gates.CanonicalHash` pin**, so the door hands over a hash the route it names will accept; finished ⇒ the follow-up route under the `revision` preset), follow-up, and the two preview routes. A closed door still ships, carrying WHY and naming the verb it will use when it opens — no dead door, no missing door. **No new verb**: a convenience endpoint dispatching between the two landed revision doors would be a second route to acts that already have one apiece, which is the double-mint shape in transport form. `acceptable()` is the ONE expression of the accept's preconditions, read by both the card and the verb, so they cannot disagree.
- **OQ4 — the merge card is return-only, and its three options say they have no executor.** The card is served VERBATIM from `internal/accept` as 200-class data (a collision is an answer, never a refusal and never a silent overwrite), alongside a per-option block: agent-auto-resolve has no executor at v0 (an unbuilt S07-seam rework), resolve-manually has none by design (it is a human act the platform does not perform), and abort-to-new-attempt has none either — but the LANDED follow-up door reaching the same outcome is named on the option. The response also states the durability posture as data: v0 keeps no merge-card store, a card that reached the execute phase left a FAILED effect queryable in the journal by its effect_id, and one caught at the applies-cleanly gate proposed no effect at all.
- **What the accept pin covers, and what it deliberately does not.** `payload_hash` = `gates.CanonicalHash` over `{deliverable_id, revision_n, content_pin, trailers}` — what the reviewer SAW about the thing being pushed. The project's expect-sha is NOT in it: HEAD moving between the read and the accept is a COLLISION, which S13.6 already answers with a merge card, and pinning HEAD would turn every concurrent accept into a 409 the reviewer cannot act on. The stale-pin refusal carries the FRESH card back, and its probe re-reads the world to prove nothing fired — no push, no proposed effect, no state move — **and no step-up consumed**.
- **⚠ DEVIATION, declared: the hash pin is checked BEFORE the PIN step-up.** The brief's R14 lists step-up first. The landed approvals order is the opposite (`checkPin` → `stepUp`, approvals.go), and it is the right one here for its own reason: a successful step-up appends an `auth.reprompt` row, and a row recording an authorization for an act that then 409s would claim an approval that never took — the §39 drain-D6 principle. It also costs a reviewer with a stale card a re-read rather than a PIN prompt they cannot use. Every R14 outcome the rubric names still holds (missing PIN ⇒ `pin_required`, wrong PIN ⇒ refused + `auth.reprompt_failed`, stale pin ⇒ 409 + fresh card + nothing fired); the ordering is the only difference, and it is asserted.
- **⚠ REPORT — `accept.Accepter` has no no-attribution path, so a revision without platform provenance is REFUSED rather than pushed.** The trailer inputs are platform facts read off the revision's minting run: engine from `engine_sessions.substrate`, model from the settled `routing.decided` record, and the vendor address derived STRUCTURALLY from the lane (`<lane>@vendor.noreply.sinet.invalid` — the platform never invents a mailbox at a company it does not speak for, RFC 2606 `.invalid` guarantees non-delivery, and the form mirrors the landed `internal/project` committer noreply). When neither resolves, `accept.RenderTrailers` would still render `Co-Authored-By:  <>` — a permanent commit whose co-author line names nobody, which is the exact mis-attribution failure S13.6 step 3 exists to prevent. The card therefore serves `acceptable:false` with the absence stated, and the verb answers 400. **The narrow gap is in `internal/accept`, not here**: `Accept` has no way to say "this one has no machine co-author". Widening it is a REFERRAL, not something this packet did.
- **The accept is AUTHORSHIP, not visibility.** The scope on the verb drops the operator limb (the `answerAsk` construction, D10): the operator can READ another owner's accept card and still cannot accept it, because the accept is that person's own outward act pushed with their own credentials (D9; one GitHub account per human). Asserted in all three directions, with the owner's own accept as the non-tautological control.
- **The transport owner-scopes the preview surface, because the manager is not an authorization layer.** `Manager.List()` returns every live session and `Stop` takes no actor — by design, §25 — so `GET /api/previews` filters by `Session.User` (member: own; operator: all) and the stop verb resolves own-or-operator with 404 before 403. Launch visibility IS deliverable read visibility. **Low tier throughout**: no journal, no proposal, no PIN, on any preview route.
- **Every preview disposition is a 200 ANSWER.** no-preview, self-preview, requires-container-tier, unavailable and at-capacity are states with machine-readable reasons, never errors — a 500 where the honest answer is "this type has no preview" is the broken-iframe failure S13.8 names. Non-backed dispositions hold no port, store no session and emit NOTHING, asserted as an absence. The at-capacity state is reached by driving the ⚙ cap to 1 through the narrow `preview.Settings` seam rather than by racing the ratified default.
- **The host-hazard discipline is extended, not assumed.** Every api-level preview fixture composes the manager with `NewCaddyClient("", "")` (routing disabled), asserted structurally — a live session's URL is a loopback backend and `Routed` is false, so a fixture that ever wired a real admin endpoint would fail. The internal/preview AST tripwire is extended over **every** `*_test.go` in `internal/api` (string literals only, so the host-hazard warnings in comments are not flagged), with a file count, a literal count and a self-probe. This package's portpool range is **47900-47919** — disjoint from internal/preview's 47700-47719 and internal/shell's 47800-47819, because the OS loopback table is the one resource no file-stub sees.
- **Content is served UNWRAPPED and the redaction edge does not widen.** Diff text, comment bodies, revision pins and deliverable rows are S13 product read from S13's OWN tables — content, not an observability projection lifted out of a `run_events` payload (§38 ruling (b); §40). No file in this packet references `internal/redact`, so `redactionEdgeFiles` stays at **three** and its enumeration stays honest. The line-diff surface ships the host-side UNIFIED GIT DIFF text and nothing else — no HTML, no tokenized form, no server-rendered highlight — because react-diff-view parses unified diffs natively and highlights client-side, and no server-side highlighter exists in the platform (FC-v1 §2); the test asserts the absence of markup, not just the presence of a diff.
- **The negatives, with probes.** `internal/api` imports neither `internal/broker` nor `internal/project` (AST import scan with a file-count floor and a self-probe), so the accept exits through `accept.Accepter` and nowhere else and the protected ref is read as one column off the S13.7 registry row rather than through an import. A second canonicalization is banned by name, every `PayloadHash` producer is asserted to reach `gates.CanonicalHash`, and the files that hash bytes at all are an ENUMERATION honest in both directions — today exactly `projection.go`, whose sha256 is the S14.3 arg fingerprint served INSTEAD of raw args and pins no answer. The every-mutation walk drives comment create, a backed preview launch and its stop, a non-backed launch, and the accept, asserting each landed canonical row and **zero `decision.recorded`**.
- **⚠ REPORT — a pre-existing mismatch found while grounding, NOT touched.** `internal/api/approvals.go`'s `cardShape.Verbs` reads `json:"verbs"`, but the landed producer of a verify escalation card (`internal/verify/escalate.go` marshalling `verify.Card`) writes its verb list under `json:"choices"`; no producer in the tree writes `verbs`. The consequence is that verify escalation cards list no actions on the inbox. `approvals.go` is B6-2A's landed surface and outside this packet's scope, so nothing was changed; this packet reads `choices` itself, citing the producer, for its request-revision door. Referral line for the coordinator.
- **Drain-round record (2026-07-29, appended at landing — the report above is RESOLVED):** the evaluation confirmed the mismatch (producer escalate.go + validator answer.go both speak top-level `choices`; nothing writes `verbs`), and the coordinator ratified a sanctioned-narrow outside-range fix in drain r1 (`1d8122e`): the parse tag moved `verbs`→`choices`, the wrong comment was corrected, and a producer-shaped regression test asserts the derived card lists its choices. The re-check swept every ask-snapshot producer: verify.Card's flat list is the ONLY top-level `choices` in any stored snapshot (intake's choices nest under `decision`), so no unmarshal regression is reachable. The rest of the round: accept commits carry the display name from the held auth store (id fallback honest), the R26 walk drives the backed preview comparison, the closed request-revision door names no route, the preview tripwire scans its own file via split-token assembly, and `checkAcceptPin` gained TrimSpace parity (blank hash = 400-missing, never 409-stale).
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` **byte-unchanged, 118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), NO migration, and `internal/review`, `internal/accept`, `internal/preview`, `internal/project`, `internal/broker`, `internal/gates`, `internal/redact`, `internal/history` and `internal/settings` are byte-untouched. `internal/shell` changes by one pre-declaration and one seam line (the review store, already composed for the pipeline and the accept, is now also held by the api layer). New bounds are structural with named reasons: `deliverablesListCap` = `readPageDefault` (one read surface, one page size) and `reworkCardCap` 50 (a task has a handful of open cards, but expectation is not a bound and this read shares the one writer connection, S02.1). Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. **$0** — every test is hermetic and tier-F: host git under `t.TempDir()` with `file://` only (§23), a fake pusher, routing disabled, no engine, no seat, no network, no real route on the host. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.

### §40-C — `/api/memory`: gate-routed writes, scoped reads & the conflict edges (P3-B6-3C; S09, S15.2, S01.9)

The **third and last part** of B6-3. Parts A and B above are unchanged: this subsection records only what part C decided. Part C is the S09 content family — the scoped browse reads, the station-3 write verbs, the owner's removal rights and the S09.7 question edges. It opens **NO migration** (the knowledge schema is 0004's and the conflict edge table with it) — 0001–0019 are byte-untouched and `user_version` stays 19 — and **NO new event type**: every act it adds has a landed canonical row or a durable row of its own, so the inventory stays **91 minted / 5 declare-only / 96 registered**.

- **The gate is the only write path, and the transport's job is to reach it and to say what it said.** `api.Config.Memory *memory.Store` + `MemoryGate *memory.Gate` are held for the `*review.Store` / `*settings.Registry` reason (§39/§40-B): `internal/memory` is a leaf over storage+eventlog, and the SPLIT between the two handles is the S09.1 capability wall showing through the composition — reads on the store, every L2 write verb on the gate. `memory.go` constructs no knowledge SQL, mints no knowledge event and re-implements none of the gate's walls; a source scan bans `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE … knowledge`, `lesson_proposals`, `.Adopt(` and `knowledge_fts` outright, with a probe, and asserts the six routed gate verbs are actually called so "no SQL" cannot pass by the file doing nothing. Every refusal carries the GATE'S OWN message: `ErrOperatorRequired`/`ErrNotHuman`/`ErrNotOwner` → 403, `ErrScopeDormant`/`ErrLayerForbidden`/`ErrInvalidEntry` → 400, `ErrNotActive` → 409, unmarked → 500 — mapped on the error's TYPE, never on its text (§38).
- **OQ5 — visibility is the CONTENT line, and it deliberately diverges from the observability precedent.** A caller sees their own entries in every scope they own (S4.1), house-scope entries (house defaults address everyone, S4.5) and project-scope entries of the projects they own or are invited to (S09.6 scope membership). **The operator role bit opens nothing of another member's user-scope store**: memory is personal CONTENT, and D10 is the authority to curate the HOUSE scope, not a read into a person's own memory. §30's operator-sees-all is a rule about runs and telemetry and it does not reach here. Both directions are pinned — the owner's own rows must be PRESENT (an all-empty handler cannot pass), and the operator's list must NOT contain a member's user-scope entry — and the divergence is structural rather than policed: `Store.ListVisible` takes a viewer and a project set and has no role parameter at all, so there is no operator limb to forget to omit.
- **⚠ REPORT (RATIFIED at drain r1; a FOURTH read, `Visible`, joined them there — see the drain record) — three narrow exported READS were genuinely missing, and they landed in `internal/memory` rather than being re-derived in a transport.** The store had `ListOwned` ("everything about ME", S4.1) and no way to read the house or project scopes, and the S09.7 edges had no reader outside the assembly source. `browse.go` adds `ListVisible(BrowseQuery)` — the same three-scope predicate the server-side `knowledge_search` already applies, bounded and filterable — plus `EntryConflicts(entry)` and `Conflict(id)`, with a `Conflict` type. They are READS: the capability split is untouched, every write verb still lives on `*Gate`, and the alternative (raw SQL over `knowledge_entries` in `internal/api`) would have duplicated the column list, the row scan and the selector decoding, giving the platform two readers of one table. Declared here as a widening, not taken silently.
- **⚠ REPORT — `Gate.authorize` still does not narrow a PROJECT write to the project's people, and the registry it was waiting for has landed.** §17 reading 3 recorded "project-owner approval narrows to the registry's project owner at B4; until then a project write is owner-attributed to its actor" — and B4-2 landed the registry. Project-scope knowledge injects into EVERY run of that project (S05.4 selects by `scope_ref`), so an unscoped door would be a way to steer other people's runs. The transport therefore refuses a project write into a project the caller neither owns nor is invited to, which is scope MAPPING of exactly the kind R19 sanctions (the same fail-closed question `authorizeOwner` answers everywhere else) — but the full station-3 narrowing (the project OWNER approves) belongs in `Gate.authorize`, and widening the gate is not a transport's act. Referral line for the coordinator.
- **The tier reading, recorded: an own-store write is Medium, so there is no PIN and no batch.** S15.2's memory row and S09.4 station 3 both say own-store writes are Medium; Medium demands no step-up (that is High's, the outward-effect tier) and is never batched (S15.6). The manual human-direct write IS the individual owner approval — the person is at the surface deciding, and the gate records them as approver and verifier at that instant (the operator-import-is-the-approval precedent). The test asserts zero `auth.reprompt` rows on a create, so "no step-up" is a measured absence rather than an omission.
- **`Gate.Import` is routed, operator-only, and `Adopt` is not (the OQ5 addition).** The create body's `origin` selects the ceremony: empty or `human_direct` is the workspace entry, `imported` is the N20 house-KB day-one import where the operator's import IS the approval (S09.4/S09.10). A member naming `imported` is 403 — nobody else stamps an entry with an origin that says a curator vouched for it — and the vocabulary is closed, so `adopted_from` (the v1 browse-and-adopt surface) and `proposed_from` (the dormant station-2 drafter) are refused BY NAME rather than silently written as human_direct.
- **Removal returns the influence list; true deletion is the owner's and the transport does not soften it.** `Remove` is owner-or-operator (§17 reading 4) and hands back `influence(entry)` — the S2.9 forward trace computed as a join over what injection already logged, served verbatim, with the re-verification OFFER named as the workspace UI's act over this data (B6-6) rather than faked here. `TrueDelete` is strictly the owner (S4.1; G2 Def.9): the test puts the operator in a position where they can READ the entry and REMOVE it, and asserts the purge is still refused with the gate's own words — authorship, not visibility, the §40-B accept line applied to a second verb. The tombstone then reads honestly (id, dates, the note; no content, no title on the wire) and the response states the S09.5 HONEST LIMITS as data: git history, the pending `VACUUM INTO`, artifacts already accepted, and no weights to unlearn.
- **OQ6 — the minimal conflict surface, and the ninth inbox kind stays B6-6's.** Open edges ride the entry detail (both sides of a pair surface the same question, which is what "surfaced" means on a resource that shows one side), and `POST /api/memory/conflicts/{conflict}/resolve` routes the landed `Gate.ResolveConflict` scoped to the AFFECTED OWNER — the card is a question addressed to one person. The operator has no limb, for the same reason they have none on true deletion. Resolved-first: a repeat serves the already-closed edge back at 200 rather than a refusal (S15.2). **No inbox projection and no approvals surface changed.** The act mints nothing: the `knowledge_conflicts` row records `resolved_by` and `resolved_ts`, so the durable record of who closed the question already exists and a co-mint would be the double-mint shape (§39 OQ8) — the recorded reading behind this family's zero.
- **R22's negatives are probes, not prose.** Eleven route probes (this sentence originally read "Twelve", which miscounted them; corrected in place at drain r1 — a record correction of an error, not a dated-truth append) assert that proposals, adoption, knowledge-search, an import door of its own, and PUT/PATCH/DELETE on an entry all fail to answer 200, with the real routes as the non-tautological control. The L1 wall needs no probe of its own: the `observation` kind is refused by the gate, and that refusal IS the dormancy. The every-mutation walk drives create, create-that-raises-a-conflict, new-version, resolve, remove and delete, asserting 3 `knowledge.write` + 1 `knowledge.conflict` + 1 `knowledge.remove` + 1 `knowledge.delete`, **zero `decision.recorded`** and zero `knowledge.curation` (that event is the S09.8 assembly-time card's, not this surface's). The route inventory's bodies are deliberately incomplete so the reachability walk lands nothing, and the walk asserts that too before counting.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` **byte-unchanged, 118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), NO migration (**0001–0019 byte-untouched**), and `internal/review`, `internal/accept`, `internal/preview`, `internal/project`, `internal/broker`, `internal/gates`, `internal/settings`, `internal/redact`, `internal/history` and `internal/memory`'s gate, store, source and seed files are byte-untouched (`internal/memory` gains ONE new file of reads). `internal/shell` changes by one pre-declaration, one assignment beside the landed gate construction and one seam pair. New bounds are structural with named reasons: `memoryListCap` = `readPageDefault` (one read surface, one page size), `projectRegistryCap` = `readPageMax` (a household-scale registry read on the one writer connection, S02.1) and `entryConflictsCap` 200 (an entry has a handful of open edges, but expectation is not a bound). Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. Entry content, titles and conflict questions are CONTENT served UNWRAPPED (§38 ruling (b)) — read from S09's own tables, never lifted from a `run_events` payload — so `redactionEdgeFiles` stays at **three** and the never-percent scan is extended over every new shape with a money-shaped guard as well. **$0** — every test is hermetic and tier-F: the contradiction screen is nil (the advisory pass is a named seam whose absence never suppresses the deterministic detection) and the git committer is nil, so nothing spawns a process, dials a network, prices a call or reaches a model. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.
- **Drain-round record (2026-07-29, appended at landing — both REPORTS above are SETTLED).** The evaluation verified the core and ratified the `browse.go` reads; the `Gate.authorize` project gap is confirmed real-but-contained and carries to the B6 gate list, now joined by `ResolveConflict`, whose authority has the identical shape (the gate checks only that the actor is a person; the affected-owner scoping is the transport's). No code moved for either. Six items applied. **D1 — the member-import wall's test was tautological in the direction that matters**: the landed case used a HOUSE body, so the gate's own `ErrOperatorRequired` answered 403 whether or not the transport's origin wall existed — with the wall disabled, a member's `POST /api/memory` carrying `{"scope":"user","origin":"imported"}` LANDS an entry whose provenance claims a curator vouched for it. The user-scope case is now asserted with a knowledge-row-count probe, and it fails on the wall-disabled code. **D2 — a conflict edge over-shared across owners.** An edge carries the other entry's id, kind, version and question text, and a house or project entry is read by many people, so serving its edges to every viewer handed a member's personal-entry metadata to readers who cannot act on it. Detail edges now filter to the AFFECTED OWNER through `mayAnswerConflict`, which the resolve verb reads too — one predicate, so the card and the door cannot diverge (the §39 D9 shape). Three-way pinned on both fixture shapes (a project entry all three read; a house entry with an edge naming a member's personal one). The consequence is recorded rather than hidden: a writer whose write raised a question addressed to SOMEONE ELSE no longer sees that edge, because the S09.7 card is the addressee's. **D3 — the visibility predicate existed twice**, as SQL in `ListVisible` and as Go in the transport's `memoryVisible`; hand-maintained twins drift, and the copy that drifts decides whether a person reads another person's memory. One `visiblePredicate` builder now serves both `ListVisible` and a new single-entry `Store.Visible`, and `memoryVisible` delegates — the transport holds no copy of the rule. Pinned by an agreement assertion over every viewer × entry, which fails when either side is hand-edited. **D4** — the probe count above, corrected in place. **D5** — the never-percent/money walk now covers `MemoryDeleted` and `MemoryConflictResolved` too, with a guard that the walk actually reached the tombstone, influence and conflict shapes. **D6** — three walls that existed but were unpinned: a co-member superseding a project entry they can READ is refused with the gate's `ErrNotOwner` while the operator limb succeeds and keeps the original owner; a member removing a house entry is refused and the entry stays active; and a repeated HTTP delete reads the tombstone back with exactly one `knowledge.delete` row, which is the idempotence the delete handler's read-back rests on.

## 41. Frontend tree, the npm adoption rail & the embedded SPA (P3-B6-4; S15.1, S15.3–S15.4, FC-v1, S16.2–S16.4, S01.4–S01.5, S01.11)

- **Repo-layout extension (§1, recorded not silent).** Two new roots: **`web/`** — the npm tree (Vite + React + TypeScript sources, configs, `package.json`, the committed `package-lock.json`), which contains **no Go file ever**; and **`internal/webui/`** — the only Go home for the frontend, which embeds the build output and serves it. `web/` is not `internal/` because it is not a Go package and nothing imports it; §1's "everything beyond `cmd/` and `tools/` is `internal/`" governs Go packages, and this is the recorded extension of its letter. Vite's `build.outDir` points at `../internal/webui/dist` with `emptyOutDir`, so the build output has exactly one home and the embed can never read a stale copy.
- **The SPA tree (resolves TBD-P3(SPA scaffold)).** Plain Vite + React 19 + TypeScript — **no meta-framework, no RSC, no SSR** [S15.1; G3 D3.3]. Scaffolded from `create-vite@9.1.1`'s react-ts template and then pinned: **every direct dependency is an exact version** in `package.json` (`^`/`~` are rejected by the gate — the §4 pin discipline applied to npm), and `package-lock.json` pins the transitive closure and IS the resolved tree. Installs are **`npm ci`, never bare `npm install`**, with **`--ignore-scripts`** (the S16.6 registry-supply-chain posture; no package in this tree needs an install script, and admitting one is a recorded decision naming the package and why). Scripts: `dev` / `build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) / `typecheck` (the same `tsc -b`, exposed bare for CI) / `test` (`vitest run`, never watch). Deliberate template deviations: **`oxlint` dropped** (a linter is a dependency and none is ratified — §2's posture on the npm side) and **`"strict": true` set explicitly** (TypeScript 6 does not default it on, and unsound app code is not a scaffold choice). **No CSS framework, no state library, no data-fetching library** — owned CSS and native `fetch`; anything more enters through the S16.4 rail.
- **Toolchain pins and what is NOT adopted.** Vite **8.1.x** (the current major, ~4.5 months old at adoption: first adoption of a settled line, not a day-one bump — the G3 D3.3 lag rider governs every major FROM here); TypeScript **6.0.x** — **TS 7.0.x, the native-compiler replacement line, is NOT adopted** (3 weeks old at adoption; a candidate for a scheduled pass, never for a scaffold); React **19.2.x**. Node and npm are HOST tools the platform cannot pin: the observed host versions are recorded in the tree entry's notes (the os-mechanism-row precedent) and CI pins its own exact node version.
- **The npm-side `modules` equivalent — the definition §4 defers to B6.** `internal/lockfile` gains one additive field, **`npm_packages`**: each entry claims the **ROOT package names** it owns. **Transitive coverage is COMPUTED, never enumerated** — the four picks pull in ~120 production packages, and a hand-listed closure would be regenerated noise burying the one addition worth reviewing. Duplicate claims across entries are rejected in `Validate`, exactly like duplicate Go `modules`.
- **The lockgate npm mechanics (`internal/lockfile/npm.go`), and the ground they rest on: the gate's graph IS the installer's graph**, so a legitimate transitive can never fail the gate.
  - **Edges followed** from every reached node: `dependencies`, `optionalDependencies`, `peerDependencies`. npm 7+ installs missing peers and platform-specific optional binaries as real nodes, so both are genuine install edges. `devDependencies` is followed from nothing — only the project root has them, and its dev tree is toolchain-scoped by classification rather than by walking. (Measured at adoption: `dependencies` alone already reaches all 121 production packages; the other two are admitted for fidelity, not to paper over a gap.)
  - **Resolution** is npm's own nearest-`node_modules` walk-up from the importer.
  - **Classification:** a package npm flags `dev` **or `devOptional`** is **toolchain-scoped wholesale** under the tree entry (`devOptional` means "also in the dev tree", e.g. `@types/react`, which is a root devDependency AND an optional peer of a pick). Everything else is the **production closure** and MUST be reachable from a claimed root; an unreachable one fails the gate **naming the package**. Optional/platform binaries need no special case — npm's own flags already give them their parent's classification.
  - **Pin lockstep:** for every frontend entry, `entry.pin` == the version the lockfile installed == the exact version `package.json` declares. Three statements, one value; a silent drift between them is how a pinned adoption stops being pinned.
  - **The tree pin is a tree IDENTITY, not a package version:** `"<vite version>+sha256:<hash of web/package-lock.json>"` (the content-hash discipline §4 already applies to vendored content, and the genai-prices entry's precedent). The gate recomputes it, so **any** dependency movement fails CI until the entry moves with it as a dated lock edit. The tree entry is therefore exempt from per-package lockstep and subject to this check instead.
  - **Absence of `web/` is a failure, not a skip:** entries claim a tree, so the tree has to exist. The gate runs both as `go run ./tools/lockgate` and inside `go test ./internal/lockfile/` over the REAL files, so plain `go test ./...` trips on npm-manifest breakage (§3).
- **Embed + serve (`internal/webui`).** `//go:embed all:dist` (the `all:` prefix keeps dotfiles, so the directive resolves on an unbuilt checkout). **Committed content is `dist/.gitkeep` and nothing else** — built assets are git-ignored, so a fresh clone compiles and `go test ./...` is green with no node build ever run. `web/public/.gitkeep` restores that file after each build (which empties the directory), so `git status` stays clean. Serve rules: hashed assets under `assets/` get `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (the name carries the content hash); `index.html` and unhashed public files get `no-store` (a deploy takes effect on reload, and the embedded FS has no modification times to revalidate against); a GET matching no asset falls back to `index.html`, which is what makes S15.11's stable URLs hard-navigable — **except under `assets/`, where a miss is a 404**, because answering a missing script with HTML turns a stale deploy into an unreadable parse error.
- **The SPA sits in FRONT of the API mux, not in it** (`internal/api/spa.go`). `/api/*` and `/events*` go to the mux unconditionally — real handlers, its 404s and its 405s alike — so **no machine path can ever be answered with HTML**, and the landed method semantics move by exactly nothing. Everything else asked for with GET or HEAD is an app route; non-GET app paths keep falling to the mux's 404. **The SPA surface is pre-session** (§9): the login page IS the SPA, and reaching it already proves tailnet membership — the same S01.9 layer-1 rationale that admits `/api/health` and the login endpoints. This widens the pre-session route CLASS; its five API members are unchanged and everything behind it stays fail-closed 401, `/events` included.
- **Honest absence.** A binary built without the SPA serves a built-in page saying **"UI not built into this binary"** and how to fix it — 200, because the control plane is serving correctly and readiness is `/api/health`'s answer, not this route's. Never a 404, never an empty shell that looks like a broken app.
- **§10's sanctioned-skip text is EXTENDED with a second class: `web-assets`.** `TestRealEmbeddedBuild` exercises the REAL embedded output and auto-runs whenever a build is present (CI always is — the web build runs before the Go steps), printing `SANCTIONED SKIP (CONVENTIONS §41, web-assets)` otherwise. It is the tier-R analog: the same "auto-run when the artifact exists" shape. The serve RULES themselves are asserted against a synthetic tree and never skip, so the skip can only ever cost the integration leg.
- **Dev-proxy posture: `VITE_SINET_ADDR` is REQUIRED, with no default.** `127.0.0.1:8482` is `shell.DefaultHTTPAddr` and, on the maintainer's host, the port the LIVE production `sinet-control` unit holds — a defaulted proxy would silently point a dev SPA at production. Unset, the dev server fails at startup naming the hazard and the recipe: `go run ./cmd/sinet --http-addr 127.0.0.1:<free-port>` then `VITE_SINET_ADDR=http://127.0.0.1:<port> npm run dev`. The dev server binds loopback only. The requirement is scoped to `command === 'serve' && !process.env.VITEST` — **vitest boots a Vite server in `serve` mode too**, and a test run must never resolve a proxy target, so builds and tests stay hermetic in a tree that has never set the variable. Nothing of Vite exists at runtime: no vite/rolldown/lightningcss code is reachable from the binary, which serves only the embedded assets.
- **CI is ONE job, deliberately ordered** (§5's workflow, extended): setup-go + setup-node (both SHA-pinned with lock entries; setup-node's OWN `cache` input keyed on `web/package-lock.json`, verified present in `action.yml` at the pinned SHA — **no separate cache action enters the rail**) → web `npm ci --ignore-scripts` / typecheck / test / build → Go gofmt / vet / build / test **over the real dist** → lock-gate. The Go steps run last so the `go:embed` integration is exercised on every run, not just the honest-absence path. Runner label stays exact; no release steps.
- **The scheduled SPA dependency pass is registered, not automated.** Conformance row `frontend-dependency-pass`, cadence `setting:frontend.dependency_pass_interval:days` read live at evaluation (passive dueness from stored state, no ticker, §32). Its S16.7 sibling `adoption.dependency_pass_months` is NAMED in the row's notes and never read, so neither pass silently reschedules the other. Registration only: no runner, no alert wiring — the pass is an operator act recorded through `RecordResult`, and a never-run row raises nothing at v0.
- **Recorded readings.**
  - **"assistant-cloud is never used"** (FC-v1/S15.4) means never **WIRED and never CALLED** — not absent from `node_modules`. It is a hard dependency of the pinned `@assistant-ui/react` and therefore sits in the tree; the negative is checkable, and its first instalment is the no-network mount probe in the harness.
  - **The "no versioned API" evidence moved.** Landed tests proved the API is unversioned by showing `/v1/...` returned 404. With the SPA fallback, every unrouted GET is an app route, so `/v1/api/...` is now answered by the app shell. The invariant is unchanged and its evidence is now stated in the SPA's terms (`assertServedByTheAppShell`); under `/api/` the 404 form is unchanged and still asserted. This is a behavior change of the packet that adds the SPA, recorded rather than absorbed.
  - **An unrouted `/api/...` path keeps the mux's own plain-text 404** rather than a JSON body. `http.ServeMux` cannot express "JSON 404 for unrouted API paths" AND "405 for a wrong method on a real route" at once — any catch-all under `/api/` that accepts a method swallows the wrong-method case. The load-bearing half (never HTML) is structural; the response shape of an unrouted machine path is left exactly as seven landed packets have had it.

### §41-B — the app shell: router, session, the one SSE client & the pick probes (P3-B6-4 part B; S15.1, S15.3, S15.11–S15.12, S01.9, FC-v1, §30)

- **The URL contract.** Real paths from day one, because they are the deep-link targets S15.11's push `navigate` field will carry: `/` mission control · `/board` · `/tasks/:id` · `/fleet` · `/inbox` · `/inbox/:id` · `/settings` · `/chat` · `/deliverables/:id` · `/workforce` · `/login`. A later packet FILLS a surface in; it never renames the route. Every non-login route ships as a named stub that states its owning packet and renders nothing from the API — honest absence, never a mocked screen that looks real. The set is pinned by test (`routes.test.ts`), so a rename is a deliberate edit in two places.
- **The router is OWNED, not adopted** (OQ6): a route table, `matchRoute`, `history.pushState`, a `popstate` listener, and an anchor component that intercepts only the plain left-click so middle-click, bookmark and hard reload behave as they would with no JavaScript. **Named door:** adopting react-router later is a rail decision like any other — it needs real B6-5..8 evidence (nested-data or loader needs this table cannot serve), an S16.4 walk, an exact pin on the **7.x** line, and gate ratification. "It is the industry standard" is not evidence.
- **One EventSource per tab, held at module scope** (`sharedStream`), so S15.3's rule is structural rather than something every future view must remember. A second subscriber JOINS the fan-out; a subscriber whose filter widens the union RE-OPENS the one connection rather than adding a second. Frames route in-app by the frame's own `topics` field, which the transport sets on every frame precisely so the unfiltered relay can be routed client-side.
- **A subscription declares the event TYPES it consumes.** Discovered fact, recorded because it shapes every future view: `EventSource` has **no catch-all listener**, and §30's contract names each delta frame with its stored event type (`event: run.state`). Somebody therefore has to name the types. Naming them in the SUBSCRIPTION, next to the view that reads them, is the one placement that cannot drift — the alternative is a hand-maintained TypeScript copy of the server's 96-type inventory, which is the §40-C D3 twin hazard in a second language.
- **Resume.** First connect is cursorless and tails from head. A transient drop is left to the browser, which retries and carries `Last-Event-ID` natively — the client does not race it. Only a FATAL close is re-opened by hand, carrying `?after_seq=<last delivered>`; with no observed cursor it re-opens cursorless rather than replaying from zero, and everyone re-snapshots. A topic (re)connect takes the server's `snapshot` frames, which REPLACE state and are never merged into a stale copy (§30: the server supersedes `Last-Event-ID` on a topic subscribe).
- **Sequence discipline — and a REPORTED deviation from the brief's literal wording.** The brief asked for a "seq-gap" signal. Against the landed server that check is not implementable honestly: `/events` drops rows the caller may not see and rows outside the filter (`sub.visible`), so **delivered sequences are legitimately sparse** and a forward jump proves nothing. A contiguity check would fire constantly and teach consumers to ignore the one signal that matters. What the client does instead: it re-snapshots whenever continuity is **NOT PROVEN** — on every (re)connect, and on any frame at or below the last delivered sequence (a rewind: the stream is not where we think it is), in which case the frame is **DROPPED rather than applied**. Both are tested, and so is the non-tautological control: an ordinary forward gap is delivered and does NOT fire the signal. **No server-side eviction signal exists**; if S14 retention ever adds one, this is where it wires in.
- **Snapshot debt is PER SUBSCRIBER** (drain r1, D3). `onResnapshot(reason, done)` hands each subscriber a `done` bound to itself, and that is the only way to clear its debt — one view cannot declare another caught up. The stream reports `live` only when NOBODY owes a re-snapshot; a subscriber that unsubscribes takes its debt with it rather than wedging the indicator. Without this, the first B6-5 view to finish its REST re-read would flip the indicator to "live" while a second view was still showing pre-gap state — exactly the stale-poses-as-live failure S15.12 forbids.
- **An unreadable delta announces itself** (drain r1, D4). Dropping a frame we cannot parse is right — the transport is not a place to invent state — but under the sparse-sequence rule above the next frame's check CANNOT notice the hole, so a silent drop would be the client's one unannounced data loss. The drop now fires `frame-unreadable` and consumers re-read. An unreadable SNAPSHOT frame needs no signal: its subscriber simply keeps owing one, which already keeps the stream out of `live`.
- **The three answers to a fatal close are different facts** (S15.12), so the client asks rather than guesses: session gone → `logged-out` (and no retry — retrying a 401 forever is noise); the probe itself failing → `unreachable` (the host is asleep or the tailnet is down); session fine → `disconnected` and a manual re-open. The indicator renders all six states with distinct labels and never lets a non-live state read as live.
- **Reconnect backoff** is a structural constant with a named reason (no ⚙ key exists and the browser cannot read the registry before login): `1s, 2s, 5s, 10s, 30s` capped — 1s first so a just-restarted control plane is picked up almost immediately, 30s ceiling so a host that slept for hours is not hammered on wake while a returning tab still reconnects inside half a minute. A connection that actually opened resets the ladder.
- **Session (S01.9, §9).** Identity comes from `GET /api/auth/session` and from nowhere else; the cookie is HttpOnly so JS never reads it, and **nothing identity-shaped is written to localStorage or sessionStorage** (asserted). The login page is the picker from `GET /api/auth/users`, the PIN as a `type=password` field cleared on submit, and the device hint as a **prefill only** — completing a login from a grant stays the server's call. Empty user list opens the bootstrap window (`POST /api/auth/users`, role operator, the first-run path). No session outside `/login` redirects with a `next` return-to; `next` is honoured only when it is a rooted same-origin path, so a crafted link cannot make the shell an open redirect.
- **Escape-by-default is a scan with an EMPTY allowlist** (`escape-scan.test.ts`): no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `eval(` or `new Function` anywhere under `web/src`, and a planted-probe test proves the scanner can fail. **CORRECTED (drain r1, D2):** the original text here claimed the scanner "scans itself too". It does not — `import.meta.glob` **excludes the importing module**, so the scanner's own file is the one file under `src/` the scan cannot see, and the concatenation trick does not change that.
